and let
E_{\sigma}
be the exceptional divisor for the blowup
\sigma_{\pi}
. The kernel of the composition

\mathscr{E}^{1}:=ker(\mathscr{E}^{0}\rightarrow\mathscr{E}^{0}|_{\mathscr{C}\times_{S}E_{\sigma}}\xrightarrow{f_{\mathcal{L}}}\mathrel{\mkern-14.0mu}\rightarrow(1,\sigma_{\pi})^{*}\mathscr{L}_{\mathcal{L}}^{0})

restricts to the Poincaré bundle used to construct the morphism
\Phi_{\mathcal{L}_{s}}
for
\mathcal{L}_{s}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}_{s}
in
[
5
, Theorem 1]
, for all
s\in S
.
∎

Let

be the universal Brill-Noether locus of bundles

, put

and

is a family of degree

We replace the family
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{C}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
with the family
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{J}}

where

, and

.

The Poincaré bundle

on

Morphisms between objects

such that

and

.

.

Remark 13
.

The induced morphism

. By Theorem

.

Lemma 4.6
.

The induced pullback maps
q_{\mathbb{P}}^{*}:A^{*}(\mathbb{P}_{J})\rightarrow A^{*}(\mathbb{P}_{\xi}),q_{B}^{*}:A^{*}(B_{J})\rightarrow A^{*}(B_{*})
are isomorphisms.

Proof.

Let
\mathbb{P}_{3,2}
be the coarse moduli space of
\mathbb{P}_{J}
. The pullback maps
A^{*}(B_{2,3}^{2})\rightarrow A^{*}(B_{J}),A^{*}(\mathbb{P}_{3,2})\rightarrow A^{*}(\mathbb{P}_{J})
are isomorphisms by Remark
12
.
The morphisms
B_{*}\rightarrow B_{2,3}^{2},\mathbb{P}_{\xi}\rightarrow\mathbb{P}_{3,2}
are finite. Consequently,
q_{\mathbb{P}}^{*},q_{B}^{*}
are injective by Remark
2
.
Applying excision on
B_{*}
, it is enough to show that
q_{\mathbb{P}}^{*},q_{B}|_{J_{2}^{3}\setminus\Theta_{3}}^{*},q_{B}|_{\Theta_{3}}^{*}
are surjective.

By Remark
10
,
\mathbb{P}_{\xi},B_{*}|_{V},B_{*}|_{\Theta_{*}}
are projective bundles over
J_{*}(3),V,\Theta_{*}
respectively.
It is enough to show that the respective relative hyperplane divisors are in the images of the corresponding pullbacks in codimension
1
by the projective bundle formula.
We prove the claim for
q_{\mathbb{P}}^{*}
. Other pullbacks shall follow similarly.

Applying
[
26
, Proposition 6.6]
and
[
24
, §6.4]
, let
\mathscr{J}ac_{3,2}\rightarrow J_{2}^{3}
be the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-gerbe given by twice the cohomology class of the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-gerbe
\mathscr{J}_{3,2}\rightarrow J_{2}^{3}
. Consequently, the induced map
\mathscr{J}_{3,2}\rightarrow\mathscr{J}ac_{3,2}
is a
\mu_{2}
-gerbe.
We have a trivialization of
\mathscr{J}ac_{3,2}
over
J_{2}^{3}
due to the
2
-torsion of the Brauer class of
\mathscr{J}_{3,2}
over
J_{2}^{3}
. That is, we have a section
\sigma_{\mathscr{J}}:J_{2}^{3}\rightarrow\mathscr{J}ac_{3,2}
.

Let
\mathbb{P}_{\mathscr{J}ac}
be the pullback of
\mathbb{P}_{J}
over
\mathscr{J}ac_{3,2}
. The section
\sigma_{\mathscr{J}}
pulls back to a section
\sigma_{\mathbb{P}J}:\mathbb{P}_{J}\rightarrow\mathbb{P}_{\mathscr{J}ac}
of
t_{\mathscr{J}}:\mathbb{P}_{\mathscr{J}ac}\rightarrow\mathbb{P}_{J}
giving us the following diagram where all squares are Cartesian

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}_{\xi}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}_{\mathscr{J}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}_{\mathscr{J}ac}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}_{J}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J_{*}(3)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{J}_{3,2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{J}ac_{3,2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J_{2}^{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J_{2}^{3}.\times B\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\square}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\square}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{\mathscr{J}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma_{\mathbb{P}J}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\square}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma_{\mathscr{J}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

The section
\sigma_{\mathbb{P}J}
defines a trivialization of the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-gerbe
\mathbb{P}_{\mathscr{J}ac}/\mathbb{P}_{J}
.
The induced map
\mathbb{P}_{\mathscr{J}}\rightarrow\mathbb{P}_{\mathscr{J}ac}
is a
\mu_{2}
-gerbe.
Therefore, the pullback map
q_{\mathbb{P}}^{*}
factors as:

A^{*}(\mathbb{P}_{J})\xleftrightarrow[t_{\mathscr{J}}^{*}]{\sigma_{\mathbb{P}J}^{*}}A^{*}(\mathbb{P}_{\mathscr{J}ac})\simeq A^{*}(\mathbb{P}_{\mathscr{J}})\rightarrow A^{*}(\mathbb{P}_{\xi})

We show that the pullback map
q_{\mathbb{P}}^{*}
is an isomorphism in codimension
1
by showing that
\dim A^{1}(\mathbb{P}_{\xi})=\dim A^{1}(\mathbb{P}_{J})
.
By the projective bundle formula, we have

\dim A^{1}(\mathbb{P}_{\mathscr{J}ac})=\dim A^{1}(\mathbb{P}_{\mathscr{J}})=\dim A^{1}(\mathscr{J}_{3,2})+1=3,\dim A^{1}(\mathbb{P}_{\xi})=\dim A^{1}(J_{*}(3))+1=2

Using
\mathbb{P}_{\mathscr{J}ac}\simeq\mathbb{P}_{J}\times B\mathbb{G}_{m}
and
A^{*}(B\mathbb{G}_{m})=\mathbb{Q}[c_{1}]
, we get

A^{1}(\mathbb{P}_{\mathscr{J}ac})\simeq A^{1}(\mathbb{P}_{J})\oplus A^{1}(B\mathbb{G}_{m})\implies\dim A^{1}(\mathbb{P}_{J})=\dim A^{1}(\mathbb{P}_{\mathscr{J}ac})-\dim A^{1}(B\mathbb{G}_{m})=3-1=2

Thus,
q_{\mathbb{P}}^{*}
is an isomorphism in codimension
1
as claimed.
∎

4.3.
Chow rings of
{\mathscr{C}_{J},\mathbb{P}_{\xi}\text{ and }\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}}

We start with
A^{*}(C_{J})
, and then use Grothendieck-Riemann-Roch Theorem, Theorem
2.3
to calculate
A^{*}(\mathbb{P}_{\xi})
and
A^{*}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi})
. Let
\mathcal{C}_{2}
be the universal curve over
\mathcal{M}_{2}
.

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
\tilde{\psi}_{i}:=\pi_{i}^{*}\mathcal{K}=2\pi_{i}^{*}\sigma_{1}
for
i=1,2
where
\pi_{i}:\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}_{*}
is the projection onto the
i
-th factor; and let
\Delta
be the diagonal. We have

A^{*}(\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*})=\mathbb{Q}[\tilde{\psi}_{1},\tilde{\psi}_{2},\Delta]/(\tilde{\psi}_{1}^{2},\tilde{\psi}_{2}^{2},2\Delta\tilde{\psi}_{1}-\tilde{\psi}_{1}\tilde{\psi}_{2},\Delta(\tilde{\psi}_{1}-\tilde{\psi}_{2}),2\Delta^{2}+\tilde{\psi}_{1}\tilde{\psi}_{2}).

Proof.

Let
Z_{W}:=\mathcal{W}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}
be the locus of Weierstrass points in
\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}
with respect to the projection onto the second factor and let
\tilde{\Delta}
be the image of the diagonal under the hyperelliptic involution with respect to the projection onto the first factor.

We use the stratification
\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}=(\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}\setminus(\Delta\cup\tilde{\Delta}\cup Z_{W}))\cup\Delta\cup\tilde{\Delta}\cup Z_{W}
along with

A^{*}(\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}\setminus(\Delta\cup\tilde{\Delta}\cup\mathcal{W}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}))=\mathbb{Q}

as shown in Lemma
3.4
.
We have the following pushforwards of total Chow groups.

•

i_{W*}(A_{*}(Z_{W}))=\mathbb{Q}.\tilde{\psi}_{1}\oplus\mathbb{Q}.\tilde{\psi}_{1}\tilde{\psi}_{2}
for the embedding
i_{W}:Z_{W}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}
.

•

i_{\Delta*}(A_{*}(\mathscr{C}_{*}))=\mathbb{Q}.[\Delta]\oplus\mathbb{Q}.\tilde{\psi}_{1}\tilde{\psi}_{2}
for the embedding
i_{\Delta}:\Delta\hookrightarrow\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}
.

•

i_{\tilde{\Delta}*}(A_{*}(\mathscr{C}_{*}))=\mathbb{Q}.[\tilde{\Delta}]\oplus\mathbb{Q}.\tilde{\psi}_{1}\tilde{\psi}_{2}
for the embedding
i_{\tilde{\Delta}}:\tilde{\Delta}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}

Thus,
A^{2}(\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*})=<\tilde{\psi}_{1}\tilde{\psi}_{2}>
. Since restriction to every fiber over
\pi_{2}
is trivial, we have
\Delta+\tilde{\Delta}-\tilde{\psi}_{1}\in\pi_{2}^{*}(A^{1}(\mathscr{C}_{*}))
. Thus,

\exists a\in\mathbb{Q}:\Delta+\tilde{\Delta}=\tilde{\psi}_{1}+a\tilde{\psi}_{2}.

The
\mu_{2}
-action of transposing coordinates in
\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}
leaves
\Delta,\tilde{\Delta}
invariant and transposes
(\tilde{\psi}_{1},\tilde{\psi}_{2})
. It follows that
a=1
.

Additionally,
\tilde{\Delta}.\tilde{\psi}_{i}=\Delta.\tilde{\psi}_{i}=\dfrac{1}{2}\tilde{\psi}_{1}\tilde{\psi}_{2}
since
\tilde{\Delta}.\pi_{i}^{*}\sigma_{1}=\Delta.\pi_{i}^{*}\sigma_{1}=[\sigma_{1}\times_{M_{*}}\sigma_{1}]\hookrightarrow\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}
for
i=1,2
and

\tilde{\Delta}.\Delta=i_{\Delta*}(\mathcal{W}_{*})=6[\sigma_{1}\times_{M_{*}}\sigma_{1}]=\dfrac{6}{4}\tilde{\psi}_{1}\tilde{\psi}_{2}\implies\Delta^{2}=-\dfrac{1}{2}\tilde{\psi}_{1}\tilde{\psi}_{2}.

Restricting to fibers over
M_{*}
, we get

\Delta,\tilde{\psi}_{1}\tilde{\psi}_{2}\neq 0\in A^{*}(\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}).

Let
U_{\Delta}:=\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}\setminus\Delta
. We have the finite map
\beta:U_{\Delta}\rightarrow M_{2,2}
and
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in A^{1}(M_{2,2})
so that
\beta^{*}\psi_{i}=\tilde{\psi}_{i}|_{U_{\Delta}}
for
i=1,2
.
The excision sequence for
\Delta\hookrightarrow\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}
gives

\mathbb{Q}.\Delta\rightarrow A^{*}(\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*})\rightarrow A^{*}(U_{\Delta})\rightarrow 0,

which implies that
\Delta,\tilde{\psi}_{1},\tilde{\psi}_{2}
are linearly independent in
A^{1}(\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*})
since
\beta^{*}
is injective.

Therefore,
\{[\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}],\tilde{\psi}_{1},\tilde{\psi}_{2},\Delta,\tilde{\psi}_{1}\tilde{\psi}_{2}\}
form a linear basis of
A^{*}(\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*})
.
∎

Corollary 3
.

Let
\psi_{i\Delta},\psi_{i\tilde{\Delta}}
be restrictions of
\tilde{\psi}_{i}
to
\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}\setminus\Delta,\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}\setminus\tilde{\Delta}
respectively for
i=1,2
. The excision sequence on
\Delta\hookrightarrow\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}
yields

A^{*}(\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}\setminus\Delta)=\mathbb{Q}[\psi_{1\Delta},\psi_{2\Delta}]/(\psi_{1\Delta},\psi_{2\Delta})^{2},A^{*}(\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}\setminus\tilde{\Delta})=\mathbb{Q}[\psi_{1\tilde{\Delta}},\psi_{2\tilde{\Delta}}]/(\psi_{1\tilde{\Delta}},\psi_{2\tilde{\Delta}})^{2}.

Definition 5
.

We shall refer to
\tilde{\Delta}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}
, and its image in
\mathcal{C}_{2}\times_{\mathcal{M}_{2}}\mathcal{C}_{2}
, as the conjugate diagonal from hereon.

Lemma 4.8
.

For any
i,j\in\{1,2,3\}
, let
\Delta_{ij}\hookrightarrow\times_{\mathcal{M}_{2}}^{3}\mathcal{C}_{2}
be the
i,j
-diagonal and
\tilde{\Delta}_{ij}\hookrightarrow\times_{M_{*}}^{3}\mathcal{C}_{2}
be the pullback of the
i,j
-conjugate diagonal under the projection
\pi_{ij}:\times^{3}_{M_{*}}\mathcal{C}_{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}_{2}\times_{\mathcal{M}_{2}}\mathcal{C}_{2}
onto the
i,j
factors. We have

A^{*}(\times^{3}_{\mathcal{M}_{2}}\mathcal{C}_{2}\setminus(\cup_{1\leq i,j\leq 3}\tilde{\Delta}_{ij}\cup_{1\leq i<j\leq 3}\Delta_{ij}))=\mathbb{Q}.

Proof.

Using
[
7
, §2.4]
, a genus
2
curve with
3
distinct marked points
(C,p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})
, with none of the
p_{i}
’s Weierstrass or conjugates under the hyperelliptic involution can be embedded as a degree
4
plane curve with a single nodal/cuspidal singularity.

We rigidify the embedding further by setting the images of the node
N\mapsto[1:0:0],p_{1}\mapsto[0:1:0],p_{2}\mapsto[0:0:1],p_{3}\mapsto[0:1:1]
.
The equation of the image is then an element of the space
V:=\{\lambda_{1}x_{1}^{2}x_{2}^{2}+\lambda_{2}x_{1}^{2}x_{2}x_{3}+\lambda_{3}x_{1}^{2}x_{3}^{2}+x_{1}x_{2}^{3}+\lambda_{4}x_{1}x_{2}^{2}x_{3}+\lambda_{5}x_{1}x_{2}x_{3}^{2}+\lambda_{6}x_{1}x_{3}^{3}+x_{2}(x_{2}-x_{3})x_{3}^{2}\}
.

With the blowup construction as described in Lemma
3.4
, we get a map
U^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\tau}\times_{\mathcal{M}_{2}}^{3}\mathcal{C}_{2}
. Shrinking
U^{\prime}
if necessary, we can ensure that
Im(\tau)=\times^{3}_{\mathcal{M}_{2}}\mathcal{C}_{2}\setminus(\cup_{1\leq i,j\leq 3}\tilde{\Delta}_{ij}\cup_{1\leq i<j\leq 3}\Delta_{ij})
. Therefore,
\tau
is a finite, surjective map from
U^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{A}^{6}
onto
Im(\tau)
. The lemma now follows from Remark
2
.
∎

Definition 6
.

Let
\mathcal{K}_{1}\hookrightarrow J_{*}(3)
be the cycle given by composing the embeddings
\sigma_{1}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{C}_{*}\xrightarrow{\Theta_{*}}J_{*}(3)
.

Theorem 4.4
.

Let
S^{*}(\mathscr{C}_{J})
be the image of the pullback
q_{C}^{*}:A^{*}(C_{J})\hookrightarrow A^{*}(\mathscr{C}_{J})
. We have

S^{*}(\mathscr{C}_{J})\simeq\mathbb{Q}[K_{\pi_{J}},\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*},\xi]/(\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}^{3},K_{\pi_{J}}^{2},\xi^{2}+K_{\pi_{J}}\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*},\xi K_{\pi_{J}},(\xi-\dfrac{3}{2}K_{\pi_{J}})\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}^{2}).

Proof.

We start by showing that
\xi,K_{\pi_{J}},\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}
are contained in
S^{*}(\mathscr{C}_{J})
. The divisors
K_{\pi_{J}},\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}
are contained in the image of
q_{C}^{*}
since
q_{J}^{*}
is an isomorphism by Theorem
3.3
.

Following the notation in
[
26
, §6]
, the map
\mathscr{C}_{J}\rightarrow C_{J}
lifts to
\mathscr{C}_{J}\rightarrow\mathcal{J}ac_{3,2,1}
(relating our notation to
[
26
]
, we have
C_{J}=\mathcal{J}_{3,2,1}
) and
\xi^{\otimes 2}\in q_{C}^{*}(Pic(C_{J}))
by
[
26
, Proposition 6.6]
.
Thus,
\xi\in S^{1}(\mathscr{C}_{J})
.

We have sections
J_{*}(3)\xrightarrow{\pi^{*}\sigma_{1}}\mathscr{C}_{J},\mathscr{C}_{*}\xrightarrow{\sigma_{J}}\mathscr{C}_{J}
given by pullbacks of the section
\sigma_{1}:M_{*}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}_{*}
. Therefore
\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}^{i}K_{\pi_{J}}^{j}=\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}^{i}\pi_{\mathscr{C}}^{*}K_{\pi}^{j}\neq 0\forall 0\leq i\leq 2,0\leq j\leq 1
and are linearly independent in
A^{*}(\mathscr{C}_{J})
.

The rigidification of
\xi
along
\pi^{*}\sigma_{1}
gives

\xi.\pi^{*}\sigma_{1}=0\implies\xi.K_{\pi_{J}}=0.

The Poincaré property of
\xi
implies that the restrictions of
(\xi-K_{\pi_{J}}-\pi^{*}\sigma_{1})
are trivial on fibers over
\mathcal{K}_{1}\simeq M_{*}
. Since
\Theta_{*}^{2}=2[\pi^{*}\sigma_{1}]
, we have

(\xi-K_{\pi_{J}}-\pi^{*}\sigma_{1}).\mathcal{K}_{1}=0\implies(\xi-\dfrac{3}{2}K_{\pi_{J}})\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}^{2}=0.

We define the map
\Sigma:\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}\setminus\tilde{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}_{J}\setminus\sigma_{J}
as
\pi_{1}\times(\phi\otimes\sigma_{1})
, where
\phi:\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}\rightarrow J_{*}(3)
was defined in §
3.2.2
and
\sigma_{J}:=\pi_{J}^{-1}(\mathcal{K}_{1})
.
The Poincaré property of
\xi
shows that
[\Sigma(\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}\setminus\tilde{\Delta})],\xi-\pi^{*}\sigma_{1}\in A^{1}(\mathscr{C}_{J}\setminus\sigma_{J})
restrict to identical divisors on fibers over
J_{*}(3)\setminus\mathcal{K}_{1}
. Thus,

\exists a\in\mathbb{Q}:[\Sigma(\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}\setminus\tilde{\Delta})]-\xi+\pi^{*}\sigma_{1}=a\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}\implies[\Sigma(\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}\setminus\tilde{\Delta})]\pi^{*}\sigma_{1}=a\pi^{*}\sigma_{1}\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}.

Transversality of the loci
\pi^{*}\sigma_{1},\Sigma(\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}\setminus\tilde{\Delta})
in
\mathscr{C}_{J}\setminus\sigma_{J}
shows that

[\Sigma(\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}\setminus\tilde{\Delta})]\pi^{*}\sigma_{1}=\pi^{*}\sigma_{1}\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}\implies a=1.

Therefore,

(10)

[\Sigma(\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}\setminus\tilde{\Delta})]=\xi-\dfrac{1}{2}K_{\pi_{J}}+\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}.

We calculate
S^{*}(\mathscr{C}_{J}\setminus\sigma_{J})
and then use the excision sequence

A^{*-2}(\sigma_{J})\xrightarrow{i_{J*}}S^{*}(\mathscr{C}_{J})\rightarrow S^{*}(\mathscr{C}_{J}\setminus\sigma_{J})\rightarrow 0.

The image of the pushforward
i_{J*}A^{*}(\sigma_{J})
, is spanned by
\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}^{2},\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}^{2}K_{\pi_{J}}
.

We define the morphism
\Phi_{\mathscr{C}}:\times^{3}_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}_{J}=\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}J_{*}(3)
as
\pi_{1}\times((\phi\circ\pi_{23})\otimes\sigma_{1})
, where
\pi_{23}:\times^{3}_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}
is the projection map onto the second and third factors.

The restriction
\phi_{\mathscr{C}}:\times^{3}_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}\setminus\tilde{\Delta}_{23}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}_{J}\setminus\sigma_{J}
is then a finite, flat map between smooth varieties. Hence,
S^{*}(\mathscr{C}_{J}\setminus\sigma_{J})
is linearly generated by
[\mathscr{C}_{J}\setminus\sigma_{J}],\phi_{\mathscr{C}*}(A_{*}(\Delta_{12}\setminus\tilde{\Delta}_{23})),\phi_{\mathscr{C}*}(A_{*}(\Delta_{23}\setminus\tilde{\Delta}_{23})),\phi_{\mathscr{C}*}(A_{*}(\tilde{\Delta}_{12}\setminus\tilde{\Delta}_{23})),\phi_{\mathscr{C}*}(A_{*}(\tilde{\Delta}_{22}\setminus\tilde{\Delta}_{23})),\text{ and }\phi_{\mathscr{C}*}(A_{*}(\tilde{\Delta}_{11}\setminus\tilde{\Delta}_{23}))
by Lemma
4.8
and Lemma
4.7
.

Using (
10
) and Corollary
3
, we have the following.

•

\phi_{\mathscr{C}*}(A^{*}(\Delta_{12}\setminus\tilde{\Delta}_{23}))
: Is the image of the map
\phi_{\mathscr{C}}\circ\Delta_{12}:\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}\setminus\tilde{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}_{J}\setminus\sigma_{J}
.

Therefore,
\phi_{\mathscr{C}*}(A^{*}(\Delta_{12}\setminus\tilde{\Delta}_{23}))
is spanned by
[\phi_{\mathscr{C}}(\Delta_{12}\setminus\tilde{\Delta}_{23})]=\xi-\pi^{*}\sigma_{1}+\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}=\xi-\dfrac{1}{2}K_{\pi_{J}}+\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*},[\phi_{\mathscr{C}*}\circ\Delta_{12*}(\tilde{\psi}_{1})]=K_{\pi_{J}}\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}
and
[\phi_{\mathscr{C}*}\circ\Delta_{12*}(\tilde{\psi}_{2})]=2(\xi-K_{\pi_{J}}+\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*})\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}
.

•

\phi_{\mathscr{C}*}(A^{*}(\Delta_{23}\setminus\tilde{\Delta}_{23}))
: Is the image of the map
\phi_{\mathscr{C}}\circ\Delta_{23}:\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}\setminus\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathcal{W}_{*}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}_{J}\setminus\sigma_{J}
.

Therefore,
\phi_{\mathscr{C}*}(A^{*}(\Delta_{23}\setminus\tilde{\Delta}_{23}))
is spanned by
[\phi_{\mathscr{C}}(\Delta_{23}\setminus\tilde{\Delta}_{23})]=4\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*},\phi_{\mathscr{C}*}\circ\Delta_{23*}(\Delta)=(2\xi-K_{\pi_{J}}+2\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*})\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}
and
\phi_{\mathscr{C}*}\circ\Delta_{23*}(\tilde{\psi}_{1})=4K_{\pi_{J}}\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}
.

•

\phi_{\mathscr{C}*}(A^{*}(\tilde{\Delta}_{12}\setminus\tilde{\Delta}_{23}))
: Is the image of the map
\phi_{\mathscr{C}}\circ\tilde{\Delta}_{12}:\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}\setminus\tilde{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}_{J}\setminus\sigma_{J}
.

Therefore,
\phi_{\mathscr{C}*}(A^{*}(\tilde{\Delta}_{12}\setminus\tilde{\Delta}_{23}))
is spanned by
[\phi_{\mathscr{C}}(\tilde{\Delta}_{12}\setminus\tilde{\Delta}_{23})]=2K_{\pi_{J}}+\pi^{*}\sigma_{1}-\xi-\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}=\dfrac{5}{2}K_{\pi_{J}}-\xi-\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*},[\phi_{\mathscr{C}*}\circ\Delta_{23*}(\tilde{\psi}_{1})]=K_{\pi_{J}}\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}
and
[\phi_{\mathscr{C}*}\circ\Delta_{23*}(\tilde{\psi}_{2})]=2(2K_{\pi_{J}}-\xi-\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*})\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}
.

•

\phi_{\mathscr{C}*}(A^{*}(\tilde{\Delta}_{22}\setminus\tilde{\Delta}_{23}))
:
Is the image of the map
\phi_{\mathscr{C}}\circ\tilde{\Delta}_{22}:\coprod_{1\leq i\leq 6}\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}\setminus\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\sigma_{i}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}_{J}\setminus\sigma_{J}
.
Let
\prescript{}{i}{\tilde{\psi}_{j}}:=\tilde{\psi}_{j}\in A^{1}(\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}\setminus\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\sigma_{i})
be the
\tilde{\psi}_{j}
class as defined in Lemma
4.7
for
j=1,2
, in the
i
-th element of this disjoint union.

Therefore,
\phi_{\mathscr{C}*}(A^{*}(\tilde{\Delta}_{22}\setminus\tilde{\Delta}_{23}))
is spanned by
\phi_{\mathscr{C}}(\tilde{\Delta}_{22}\setminus\tilde{\Delta}_{23})=6\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}
and
\phi_{\mathscr{C}*}\circ\tilde{\Delta}_{22*}(\prescript{}{i}{\tilde{\psi}_{1}})=K_{\pi_{J}}\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*},\phi_{\mathscr{C}*}\circ\tilde{\Delta}_{22*}(\Delta)=(\xi-K_{\pi_{J}}+\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*})\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}\forall 1\leq i\leq 6
.

•

\phi_{\mathscr{C}*}(A^{*}(\tilde{\Delta}_{11}\setminus\tilde{\Delta}_{23}))
: Is the image of the map
\phi_{\mathscr{C}}|_{\pi^{*}\sigma_{1}}:\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}\setminus\tilde{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}_{J}
.

Therefore,
\phi_{\mathscr{C}*}(A^{*}(\tilde{\Delta}_{11}\setminus\tilde{\Delta}_{23}))
is spanned by
\pi^{*}\sigma_{1}=\dfrac{1}{2}K_{\pi_{J}},\phi_{\mathscr{C}*}(\sigma_{1}\times_{M_{*}}\sigma_{1}\times_{M_{*}}(\mathscr{C}_{*}\setminus\sigma_{1})=\pi^{*}\sigma_{1}\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}=\dfrac{1}{2}K_{\pi_{J}}\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}
.

Therefore,
S^{1}(\mathscr{C}_{J})
is spanned by
\{\xi,K_{\pi_{J}},\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}\}
,
S^{2}(\mathscr{C}_{J})
is spanned by
\{\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}^{2},\xi\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*},K_{\pi_{J}}\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}\}
,
S^{3}(\mathscr{C}_{J})
is spanned by
\{K_{\pi_{J}}\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}^{2}\}
, and
S^{i}(\mathscr{C}_{J})=0
for all
i\geq 4
.

We show that
\{\xi,K_{\pi_{J}},\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}\}\in A^{1}(\mathscr{C}_{J})
are linearly independent. It has been shown that
K_{\pi_{J}},\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}
are linearly independent. Hence, it is enough to show that
\xi\not\in<K_{\pi_{J}},\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}>
.

Assuming the contrary, we must have
\xi\in\mathbb{Q}.K_{\pi_{J}}
since
\xi.K_{\pi_{J}}=K_{\pi_{J}}^{2}=0
and
K_{\pi_{J}}.\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}\neq 0
. Consequently, we have

(\xi-\dfrac{3}{2}K_{\pi_{J}}).\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}^{2}=0\implies\xi=\dfrac{3}{2}K_{\pi_{J}}.

Restriction to a general fiber over
J_{*}(3)
produces the desired contradiction and we have that
\{\xi,K_{\pi_{J}},\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}\}\in A^{1}(\mathscr{C}_{J})
are linearly independent.

Linear independence of
\{\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}^{2},\xi\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*},K_{\pi_{J}}\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}\}\in A^{2}(\mathscr{C}_{J})
follows similarly.

To describe the intersection product on
S^{*}(\mathscr{C}_{J})
, it is enough to express
\xi^{2}
in terms of
\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}^{2},\xi\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*},K_{\pi_{J}}\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}
.

We must have
\xi^{2}\in<\xi\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*},K_{\pi_{J}}\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}>
since
\xi.K_{\pi_{J}}=0
. Let
\overline{\Sigma}:\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}_{J}
be the natural extension of
\Sigma
.

Let
h
be the hyperelliptic involution on the family
\pi_{J}
and
Z:=\overline{\Sigma}(\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*})=\overline{\Sigma(\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}\setminus\tilde{\Delta})}
.

We have
[Z]=\xi-\dfrac{1}{2}K_{\pi_{J}}+\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}
by (
10
). The divisors
h^{*}(\xi)+\xi,3K_{\pi_{J}}
have identical restrictions on every fiber of
\pi_{J}
. Thus,

h^{*}(\xi)+\xi-3K_{\pi_{J}}=b\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}\text{ for some }b\in\mathbb{Q}.

Using
h^{*}(\xi).K_{\pi_{J}}=h^{*}(\xi.h^{*}(K_{\pi_{J}}))=h^{*}(\xi.K_{\pi_{J}})=0
, we have

\displaystyle bK_{\pi_{J}}\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}=0\implies b=0\implies h^{*}([Z])=\dfrac{5}{2}K_{\pi_{J}}-\xi+\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}\implies[Z]h^{*}([Z])=-\xi^{2}+2K_{\pi_{J}}\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}+\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}^{2}.

Consequently, we have

[Z]h^{*}[Z]=h^{*}([Z]h^{*}[Z])=-h^{*}(\xi^{2})+2K_{\pi_{J}}\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}+\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}^{2}\implies h^{*}(\xi^{2})=\xi^{2}.

Therefore,
\xi^{2}\in\mathbb{Q}.K_{\pi_{J}}\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}
since
\xi.\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}
is not invariant under
h^{*}
. Let
\xi^{2}=cK_{\pi_{J}}\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}
, then

[Z]h^{*}([Z])=(2-c)K_{\pi_{J}}\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}+\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}^{2}\text{ and }[Z]h^{*}([Z])\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}=(2-c)K_{\pi_{J}}\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}^{2}.

We have
[Z]h^{*}([Z])\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}=i_{*}(i^{*}[Z]i^{*}(h^{*}[Z]))
where
i:\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}\simeq{\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\Theta_{*}}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{C}_{J}
is the natural inclusion obtained from
\Theta_{*}\hookrightarrow J_{*}(1)
. Using
i
and restricting
[Z]h^{*}([Z])
, we get

i^{*}[Z]=\Delta+\sigma_{1}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}=\Delta+\dfrac{1}{2}(\tilde{\psi}_{1}+\tilde{\psi}_{2}),i^{*}(h^{*}([Z]))=\tilde{\Delta}+\dfrac{1}{2}(\tilde{\psi}_{1}+\tilde{\psi}_{2})\implies\\
i^{*}[Z]i^{*}(h^{*}[Z])=3\tilde{\psi}_{1}\tilde{\psi}_{2}\implies[Z]h^{*}([Z])\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}=i_{*}(3\tilde{\psi}_{1}\tilde{\psi}_{2})=12i_{*}(\sigma_{1}\times_{M_{*}}\sigma_{1})=3K_{\pi_{J}}\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}^{2}.

Therefore,
c=-1
and
\xi^{2}=-K_{\pi_{J}}\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}
giving all the generating relations in
S^{*}(\mathscr{C}_{J})
.
∎

Remark 14
.

The arguments in Lemma
4.8
do not extend over to the corresponding open in
\times_{M_{*}}^{3}\mathscr{C}_{*}
directly. This further limits the scope of computations in Theorem
4.4
to
C_{J}
.
Consequently, the arguments for
A^{*}(\mathscr{C}_{J})
cannot be obtained plainly from the ones in the aforementioned theorem.

From the definition of
\mathbb{P}_{\xi}
, we have
\mathbb{P}_{\xi}={Proj}_{J_{*}(3)}\operatorname{Sym}^{\bullet}(\pi_{J*}(K_{\pi_{J}}\otimes\xi))^{\vee}
.

Applying the Grothendieck-Riemann-Roch to the morphism
\pi_{J}
on
\xi\rightarrow\mathscr{C}_{J}
, we have

ch(\pi_{J*}(K_{\pi_{J}}\otimes\xi))=\pi_{J*}(ch(K_{\pi_{J}}\otimes\xi)Td(K_{\pi_{J}}^{\vee}))=\pi_{J*}((1+K_{\pi_{J}}+\xi-\dfrac{1}{2}K_{\pi_{J}}\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*})(1-\dfrac{1}{2}K_{\pi_{J}}))=4-\Theta_{*}.

Therefore,
c_{1}(\pi_{J*}(K_{\pi_{J}}\otimes\xi))=-\Theta_{*},c_{2}(\pi_{J*}(K_{\pi_{J}}\otimes\xi))=\dfrac{1}{2}\Theta_{*}^{2},c_{i}(\pi_{J*}(K_{\pi_{J}}\otimes\xi))=0\forall i\geq 3
.

Corollary 4
.

Let
H_{J}:=c_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}_{\xi}}(1))\in A^{1}(\mathbb{P}_{\xi})
and
\Theta_{\xi}:=\pi_{\xi}^{*}\Theta_{*}
be the pullback of the theta divisor on
J_{*}(3)
. We have

A^{*}(\mathbb{P}_{\xi})=\mathbb{Q}[\Theta_{\xi},H_{J}]/(\Theta_{\xi}^{3},H_{J}^{4}+H_{J}^{3}\Theta_{\xi}+\dfrac{1}{2}H_{J}^{2}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}).

Lemma 4.9
.

The total chern class of the normal bundle
\mathcal{N}_{\mathscr{C}_{J}/\mathbb{P}_{\xi}}
is given by

c(\mathcal{N}_{\mathscr{C}_{J}/\mathbb{P}_{\xi}})=1+(5K_{\pi_{J}}+4\xi+\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*})+\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}(3\xi-2K_{\pi_{J}})+\dfrac{1}{2}\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}^{2}\in A^{*}(\mathscr{C}_{J}).

Proof.

We have the Euler sequence on the projective bundle
\mathbb{P}_{\xi}\xrightarrow{\pi_{\xi}}J_{*}(3)

0\rightarrow\Omega^{1}_{\mathbb{P}_{\xi}/J_{*}(3)}\rightarrow\pi_{\xi}^{*}\pi_{J*}(K_{\pi_{J}}\otimes\xi)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}_{\xi}}(-1)\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}_{\xi}}\rightarrow 0

which restricts to
0\rightarrow\Omega^{1}_{\mathbb{P}_{\xi}/J_{*}(3)}|_{\mathscr{C}_{J}}\rightarrow\pi_{J}^{*}\pi_{J*}(K_{\pi_{J}}\otimes\xi)\otimes\underbrace{\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}_{\xi}}(-1)|_{\mathscr{C}_{J}}}_{(K_{\pi_{J}}\otimes\xi)^{\vee}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{C}_{J}}\rightarrow 0
over
\mathscr{C}_{J}
. Additionally, the conormal bundle sequence

0\rightarrow\mathcal{N}_{\mathscr{C}_{J}/\mathbb{P}_{\xi}}^{\vee}\rightarrow\Omega^{1}_{\mathbb{P}_{\xi}/J_{*}(3)}|_{\mathscr{C}_{J}}\rightarrow K_{\pi_{J}}\rightarrow 0\text{ gives }

\displaystyle c(\mathcal{N}_{\mathscr{C}_{J}/\mathbb{P}_{\xi}}^{\vee})=c(\Omega^{1}_{\mathbb{P}_{\xi}/J_{*}(3)}|_{\mathscr{C}_{J}})(1-K_{\pi_{J}})=c(\pi_{J}^{*}\pi_{J*}(K_{\pi_{J}}\otimes\xi)\otimes(K_{\pi_{J}}\otimes\xi)^{\vee})(1-K_{\pi_{J}})

\displaystyle=1-(5K_{\pi_{J}}+4\xi+\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*})+(3\xi-2K_{\pi_{J}})\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}+\dfrac{1}{2}\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}^{2}

since
c(\pi_{J*}(K_{\pi_{J}}\otimes\xi))=1-\Theta_{*}+\dfrac{1}{2}\Theta_{*}^{2}\in A^{*}(J_{*}(3))
. Therefore,
c(\mathcal{N}_{\mathscr{C}_{J}/\mathbb{P}_{\xi}})=1+(5K_{\pi_{J}}+4\xi+\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*})+\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}(3\xi-2K_{\pi_{J}})+\dfrac{1}{2}\Theta_{*}^{2}
as claimed.
∎

Lemma 4.10
.

We have the following expressions for cycles in terms of generators in
A^{*}(\mathbb{P}_{\xi})
.

[\mathscr{C}_{J}]=5H_{J}^{2}+3H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}+\dfrac{1}{2}\Theta_{\xi}^{2},[K_{\pi_{J}}]=2H_{J}^{3}+2H_{J}^{2}\Theta_{\xi}+H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}^{2},[\xi]=3H_{J}^{3}+H_{J}^{2}\Theta_{\xi}-\dfrac{1}{2}H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}

where we identify
K_{\pi_{J}},\xi\in A^{1}(\mathscr{C}_{J})
with their pushforwards in
A^{3}(\mathbb{P}_{\xi})
. Consequently,
[\pi^{*}\sigma_{i}]=\dfrac{1}{2}[K_{\pi_{J}}]=H_{J}^{3}+H_{J}^{2}\Theta_{\xi}+\dfrac{1}{2}H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}\forall 1\leq i\leq 6
.

Proof.

We start with proving the claimed expressions for
K_{\pi_{J}},\xi
. By Corollary
4
,
A^{3}(\mathbb{P}_{\xi})
is freely spanned by
H_{J}^{3},H_{J}^{2}\Theta_{\xi},H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}
.
Let
\bullet
be the intersection product in
A^{*}(\mathscr{C}_{J})
. We have

H_{J}.[K_{\pi_{J}}]=j_{*}((K_{\pi_{J}}+\xi)\bullet K_{\pi_{J}})=0\implies[K_{\pi_{J}}]=2H_{J}^{3}+2H_{J}^{2}\Theta_{\xi}+H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}

since
[K_{\pi_{J}}],2H_{J}^{3}
have identical restrictions on fibers of
\pi_{\xi}
.

Using a similar argument for
[\xi],3H_{J}^{3}
, we have

\displaystyle H_{J}.[\xi]=j_{*}((K_{\pi_{J}}+\xi)\bullet\xi)=-j_{*}(K_{\pi_{J}}\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*})=-j_{*}(K_{\pi_{J}})\Theta_{\xi}=-[K_{\pi_{J}}].\Theta_{\xi}=-(2H_{J}^{3}\Theta_{\xi}+2H_{J}^{2}\Theta_{\xi}^{2})\implies

\displaystyle[\xi]=3H_{J}^{3}+H_{J}^{2}\Theta_{\xi}-\dfrac{1}{2}H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}\implies H_{J}.[\mathscr{C}_{J}]=j_{*}(H_{J}|_{\mathscr{C}_{J}})=j_{*}(K_{\pi}+\xi)=5H_{J}^{3}+3H_{J}^{2}\Theta_{\xi}+\dfrac{1}{2}H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}.

Using Corollary
4
, it follows that
A^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{\xi})
is freely spanned by
\{H_{J}^{2},H_{J}\Theta_{\xi},H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}\}
. Therefore, we have

[\mathscr{C}_{J}]=5H_{J}^{2}+3H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}+\dfrac{1}{2}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}.

∎

Let
E_{\sigma}
be the exceptional divisor of
\sigma_{\pi}
, and
i_{\sigma}:E_{\sigma}\hookrightarrow\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}
be the inclusion map.

Following the notational setup for
\mathscr{C}_{J}
, let
S^{*}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi})=Im(\tilde{q}^{*})
where
\tilde{q}:\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}\rightarrow Bl_{C_{J}}(\mathbb{P}_{J})
is the map induced by
q_{C},q_{J}
.
Applying Theorem
2.3
to Theorem
4.4
and Corollary
4
we get the following theorem.

Theorem 4.5
.

Let
\xi_{\sigma}:=i_{\sigma*}(\sigma_{\pi}|_{E_{\sigma}}^{*}\xi),\text{ and }K_{\sigma}:=i_{\sigma*}(\sigma_{\pi}|_{E_{\sigma}}^{*}K_{\pi_{J}})
. We have

\displaystyle S^{*}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi})=\mathbb{Q}[\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J},E_{\sigma},\xi_{\sigma},\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi},K_{\sigma}]/(\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(H_{J}^{4}+H_{J}^{3}\Theta_{\xi}+\dfrac{1}{2}H_{J}^{2}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}),\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi}^{3},\xi_{\sigma}^{2}+E_{\sigma}K_{\sigma}\Theta_{\xi},E_{\sigma}K_{\sigma}-K_{\sigma}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi}+

\displaystyle\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(2H_{J}^{3}+2H_{J}^{2}\Theta_{\xi}+H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}),(\xi_{\sigma}-\dfrac{3}{2}K_{\sigma})\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi}^{2},K_{\sigma}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J},E_{\sigma}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}-(K_{\sigma}+\xi_{\sigma}),\xi_{\sigma}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}+K_{\sigma}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi},

\displaystyle E_{\sigma}^{2}-(5K_{\sigma}+4\xi_{\sigma}+E_{\sigma}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi})+\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(5H_{J}^{2}+3H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}+\dfrac{1}{2}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}),K_{\sigma}^{2},\xi_{\sigma}K_{\sigma},E_{\sigma}\xi_{\sigma}-\xi_{\sigma}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi}+4K_{\sigma}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi}+

\displaystyle\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(3H_{J}^{3}+H_{J}^{2}\Theta_{\xi}-\dfrac{1}{2}H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}))).

Proof.

Following the notational setup we have developed thus far, let
S^{*}(E_{\sigma})
by the image of the pullback map
A^{*}(\tilde{E})\rightarrow A^{*}(E_{\sigma})
where
\tilde{E}
is the exceptional divisor of the blowup of
\mathbb{P}_{J}
along
C_{J}
. By Lemma
4.9
and the projective bundle formula, we have

(11)

A^{*}(\mathbb{P}\mathcal{N}_{\mathscr{C}_{J}/\mathbb{P}_{\xi}})\supset S^{*}(E_{\sigma})=\mathbb{Q}[\sigma^{*}K_{\pi_{J}},\sigma^{*}\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*},\sigma^{*}\xi,\zeta]/(\sigma^{*}K_{\pi_{J}}^{2},\sigma^{*}\xi^{2}+\sigma^{*}(K_{\pi_{J}}\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}),\sigma^{*}(\xi K_{\pi_{J}}),\\
\sigma^{*}((\xi-\dfrac{3}{2}K_{\pi_{J}})\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}^{2}),\sigma^{*}\Theta_{\xi}^{3},\zeta^{2}+\zeta\sigma^{*}(5K_{\pi_{J}}+4\xi+\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*})+\sigma^{*}((3\xi-2K_{\pi_{J}})\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}+\dfrac{1}{2}\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}^{2}))

where
\sigma^{*}Z:=\sigma_{\pi}|_{E_{\sigma}}^{*}Z\quad\forall Z\in S^{*}(\mathscr{C}_{J})
, and
\zeta
is the relative hyperplane divisor in
E_{\sigma}\simeq Proj_{\mathscr{C}_{J}}\operatorname{Sym}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathscr{C}_{J}/\mathbb{P}_{\xi}})^{\vee}
.

Furthermore, for
Z\in S^{*}(\mathscr{C}_{J})
we have

i_{\sigma*}\sigma_{\pi}|_{E_{\sigma}}^{*}(Z\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*})=i_{\sigma*}(\sigma_{\pi}|_{E_{\sigma}}^{*}Z)\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi}.

Let
\sigma_{*}:=\sigma_{\pi}|_{E_{\sigma}*}
. Using
[
4
, Proposition 0.1.3]
, we have

z=\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\sigma_{\pi*}z-i_{\sigma*}\sigma^{*}\sigma_{*}i_{\sigma}^{*}z\forall z\in A^{*}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}).

Applying this formula to the case where
z=i_{\sigma*}(t)
for
t\in A^{*}(E_{\sigma})
gives

z=\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\sigma_{\pi*}(i_{\sigma*}t)-i_{\sigma*}\sigma^{*}\sigma_{*}i_{\sigma}^{*}i_{\sigma*}(t)=\sigma_{\pi}^{*}j_{*}\sigma_{*}t+i_{\sigma*}\sigma^{*}\sigma_{*}(\zeta t)\quad\forall t\in A^{*}(E_{\sigma}).

Using
E_{\sigma}^{2}=-i_{\sigma*}(\zeta),E_{\sigma}K_{\sigma}=-i_{\sigma*}(\zeta\sigma^{*}K_{\pi_{J}}),E_{\sigma}\xi_{\sigma}=-i_{\sigma*}(\zeta\sigma^{*}\xi)
, (
11
), and Lemma
4.10
, we get the following elements in the ideal of relations of
S^{*}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi})
:

E_{\sigma}^{2}-(5K_{\sigma}+4\xi_{\sigma}+E_{\sigma}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi})+\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(5H_{J}^{2}+3H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}+\dfrac{1}{2}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}),E_{\sigma}K_{\sigma}-K_{\sigma}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi}+\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(2H_{J}^{3}+2H_{J}^{2}\Theta_{\xi}+H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}),\\
E_{\sigma}\xi_{\sigma}-\xi_{\sigma}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi}+4K_{\sigma}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi}+\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(3H_{J}^{3}+H_{J}^{2}\Theta_{\xi}-\dfrac{1}{2}H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}),\xi_{\sigma}^{2}+E_{\sigma}K_{\sigma}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi},K_{\sigma}^{2},\xi_{\sigma}K_{\sigma},(\xi_{\sigma}-\dfrac{3}{2}K_{\sigma})\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}.

With the above relations in mind, the following equations describe
S^{*}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi})

\displaystyle i_{\sigma*}(Y)\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}^{a}\Theta_{\xi}^{b}=i_{\sigma*}(Y\sigma_{\pi}|_{E_{\sigma}}^{*}(K_{\pi_{J}}+\xi)^{a}\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}^{b})\forall a,b\geq 0,Y\in A^{*}(E_{\sigma})\implies

\displaystyle K_{\sigma}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}=0,\xi_{\sigma}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}=-K_{\sigma}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi},E_{\sigma}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}=K_{\sigma}+\xi_{\sigma}.

Since

\displaystyle\{[\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}],\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J},\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi},E_{\sigma},\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}^{2},\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi}^{2},\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}),K_{\sigma},\xi_{\sigma},E_{\sigma}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi},\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}^{3},\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(H_{J}^{2}\Theta_{\xi}),

\displaystyle\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}),E_{\sigma}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi}^{2},K_{\sigma}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi},\xi_{\sigma}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi},\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(H_{J}^{3}\Theta_{\xi}),\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(H_{J}^{2}\Theta_{\xi})^{2},K_{\sigma}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi}^{2},\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(H_{J}^{3}\Theta_{\xi}^{2})\}

are independent linear generators of
S^{*}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi})
by
[
4
, Proposition 0.1.3]
.
∎

4.4.
Some loci in the blowup

We define some loci in
\mathbb{P}_{\xi}
and consider their proper transforms in
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}
.

Let
\pi_{\phi}:\mathbb{P}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}
be the pullback of
\pi_{\xi}:\mathbb{P}_{\xi}\rightarrow J_{*}(3)
over
\phi\otimes\sigma_{1}:\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}\rightarrow J_{*}(3)
, let
\overline{\sigma}_{j}
be the pullback of the section
\pi^{*}\sigma_{j}
over
\phi\otimes\sigma_{1}
for
1\leq j\leq 6
, and let
\mathbb{P}_{\phi(\sigma_{1})}:\mathbb{P}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathbb{P}_{\xi}
be the induced map.
Put
\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i=1,2}
as the diagonal sections for the morphism
\pi_{12}:\times^{3}_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}_{\xi}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\times^{3}_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{C}_{J}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J_{*}(3).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\xi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}_{\phi(\sigma_{1})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\phi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi_{\mathscr{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{12}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\square}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{J}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi\otimes\sigma_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{i}{,}\overline{\sigma}_{j}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{*}\sigma_{j}}

We construct
L_{1}
to be the
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle over
\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}
, the fibers of which are given by lines joining the sections
\Delta_{1},\Delta_{2}
over
\pi_{\phi}
.

More concretely,
L_{1}:=\mathbb{P}_{\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}}(\pi_{\phi*}(\mathcal{F}|_{\Delta_{1}\cup\Delta_{2}}))^{\vee}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
where
\mathcal{F}:=\Phi_{\mathscr{C}}^{*}(K_{\pi_{J}}\otimes\xi)
.
That is,
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}=\mathbb{P}_{\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}}(\pi_{\phi*}(\mathcal{F}))^{\vee}
.
The embedding
L_{1}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
follows from the exact sequence

0\rightarrow\mathcal{F}(-\Delta_{1}-\Delta_{2})\rightarrow\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}|_{\Delta_{1}\cup\Delta_{2}}\rightarrow 0,

and
R^{1}\pi_{12*}(\mathcal{F}(-\Delta_{1}-\Delta_{2}))=0
.
We use the exact sequence

0\rightarrow\mathcal{F}(-\Delta_{1}-\Delta_{2}-\overline{\sigma}_{2})\rightarrow\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}|_{\Delta_{1}\cup\Delta_{2}\cup\overline{\sigma}_{2}}\rightarrow 0

to define a hyperplane bundle
H_{2}:=\mathbb{P}_{\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}}(\pi_{\phi*}(\mathcal{F}|_{\Delta_{1}\cup\Delta_{2}\cup\overline{\sigma}_{2}}))^{\vee}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
.

To see that
H_{2}
is a hyperplane bundle, we note that

R^{1}\pi_{12*}(\mathcal{F}(-\Delta_{1}-\Delta_{2}-\overline{\sigma}_{2}))=0.

This is due to the cohomology and base change theorem along with the observation that
\mathcal{F}(-\Delta_{1}-\Delta_{2}-\overline{\sigma}_{2}),\mathcal{O}(\overline{\sigma}_{1}+\overline{\sigma}_{2})
restrict to identical divisors on ever fiber of
\pi_{12}
.

Equivalently,
H_{2}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
is the hyperplane-bundle, the fiber of which over
q\in\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}
, is given by the hyperplane which contains the tangent line to
(\times_{M_{*}}^{3}\mathscr{C}_{*})_{q}
at
(\overline{\sigma}_{2})_{q}
and
\{(\Delta_{i})_{q}\}_{i=1,2}
.

Definition 7
.

Let
L_{\xi_{1}}:=\mathbb{P}_{\phi(\sigma_{1})}(L_{1}),H_{\xi_{2}}:=\mathbb{P}_{\phi(\sigma_{1})}(H_{2})
be the images of these loci in
\mathbb{P}_{\xi}
.
We define
\{H_{\xi_{i}}\}_{2\leq i\leq 6}
using
\overline{\sigma}_{i}
instead of
\overline{\sigma}_{2}
and
\{L_{\xi_{i}}\}_{2\leq i\leq 6}
analogously using the maps
\phi\otimes\sigma_{i}
instead of
\phi\otimes\sigma_{1}
.

The next locus we define is a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle over
J_{*}(3)
named
L_{2\sigma_{1}}
, the fiber of which over
p\in J_{*}(3)
is given by line tangent to
(\mathscr{C}_{J})_{p}
at the point
(\pi^{*}\sigma_{1})_{p}
.

Definition 8
.

Put
L_{2\sigma_{1}}:=\mathbb{P}_{J_{*}(3)}\pi_{\xi*}((K_{\pi_{J}}\otimes\xi)|_{2\sigma_{1}})
where the ideal of the closed subscheme
2\sigma_{1}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{C}_{J}
is given by
\mathcal{I}_{2\sigma_{1}/\mathbb{P}_{\xi}}:=\mathcal{I}_{\pi^{*}\sigma_{1}/\mathbb{P}_{\xi}}^{2}
.
The embedding
L_{2\sigma_{1}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}_{\xi}
is defined by the natural surjection
K_{\pi_{J}}\otimes\xi\twoheadrightarrow(K_{\pi_{J}}\otimes\xi)|_{2\sigma_{1}}
.

We shall also use the loci
\{L_{2\sigma_{i}}\}_{2\leq i\leq 6}
and the loci
\{L_{\sigma_{j}\sigma_{k}}\}_{1\leq j<k\leq 6}
, which are defined analogously:
\mathbb{P}_{J_{*}(3)}\pi_{\xi*}((K_{\pi_{J}}\otimes\xi)|_{X})
with
\mathcal{I}_{X}=\mathcal{I}_{\pi^{*}\sigma_{i}/\mathbb{P}_{\xi}}^{2}\text{ or }\mathcal{I}_{\pi^{*}\sigma_{j}/\mathbb{P}_{\xi}}\mathcal{I}_{\pi^{*}\sigma_{k}/\mathbb{P}_{\xi}}
respectively.

Remark 15
.

It is worth noting that
L_{2\sigma_{1}}
is a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle over
J_{*}(3)
and
L_{1}
is a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle over
\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}
.
However,
L_{\xi_{1}}
is not a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle over
J_{*}(3)
. More conceretely,
L_{\xi_{1}}
is a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle bundle to
J_{*}(3)\setminus\mathcal{K}_{1}
and
L_{\xi_{1}},Q_{\xi}
have identical restrictions over
\mathcal{K}_{1}
.

Similarly,
H_{\xi_{2}}
is not a
\mathbb{P}^{2}
-bundle over
J_{*}(3)
. It is a
\mathbb{P}^{2}
-bundle over
J_{*}(3)\setminus\mathcal{K}_{1}
and
H_{\xi_{2}},\mathbb{P}_{\xi}
have identical restrictions over
\mathcal{K}_{1}
.

We can now calculate expressions for these loci and their proper transforms in
\mathbb{P}_{\xi},\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}
.

Lemma 4.11
.

Fr all
1\leq i,j,k,l\leq 6
, we have the following expressions for the aforementioned loci in
A^{*}(\mathbb{P}_{\xi})
:

[H_{\xi_{i}}]=H_{J}+\Theta_{\xi},[Q_{\xi}]=2H_{J}+\Theta_{\xi},[L_{2\sigma_{i}}]=[L_{\sigma_{j}\sigma_{k}}]=H_{J}^{2}+H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}+\dfrac{1}{2}\Theta_{\xi}^{2},[L_{\xi_{l}}]=H_{J}^{2}+2H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}+\dfrac{1}{2}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}.

Proof.

We start with
[L_{2\sigma_{1}}]
. The loci
\{L_{2\sigma_{i}}\}_{1\leq i\leq 6}
are permuted under the
S_{6}
-action on
\mathbb{P}_{\xi}
and thus have the same class in
A^{*}(\mathbb{P}_{\xi})
since the generators
H_{J},\Theta_{\xi}
are
S_{6}
-invariant.
Put
[L_{2\sigma_{1}}]=H_{J}^{2}+aH_{J}\Theta_{\xi}+b\Theta_{\xi}^{2}
for some
a,b\in\mathbb{Q}
.

Since
L_{2\sigma_{1}},L_{2\sigma_{2}}
intersect transversally on the locus of vertices of cones in
Q_{\xi}
, and
L_{2\sigma_{1}},\pi^{*}\sigma_{2}
intersect transversally on the vertex of the cones in
Q_{\xi}
that are fibered over
\mathcal{K}_{2}
, we have
\pi_{\xi*}(L_{2\sigma_{1}}.L_{2\sigma_{2}})=\Theta_{*},\pi_{\xi*}(L_{2\sigma_{1}}.\pi^{*}\sigma_{2})=\mathcal{K}_{2}
. Hence,
2a-1=1\implies a=1,b=\dfrac{1}{2}
. Thus,

[L_{2\sigma_{1}}]=H_{J}^{2}+H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}+\dfrac{1}{2}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}.

Let
\mathcal{K}_{123}\hookrightarrow J_{*}(3)
be the locus of points in
J_{*}(3)
over which
\xi,\mathcal{O}(\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}+\sigma_{3})\in Pic(\mathscr{C}_{J})
define identical divisors.
It follows that
[\mathcal{K}_{123}]=[\mathcal{K}_{1}]=\dfrac{1}{2}\Theta_{*}^{2}
in
A^{*}(J_{*}(3))
. We have

\pi_{\xi*}(L_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}.L_{\sigma_{3}\sigma_{4}})=\Theta_{*},\pi_{\xi*}(L_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}.\pi^{*}\sigma_{3})=\mathcal{K}_{123}.

Using similar arguments as above, we get

[L_{\sigma_{j}\sigma_{k}}]=H_{J}^{2}+H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}+\dfrac{1}{2}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}\forall 1\leq j<k\leq 6.

Let
[L_{\xi_{1}}]=H_{J}^{2}+cH_{J}\Theta_{\xi}+d\Theta_{\xi}^{2}
for some
c,d\in\mathbb{Q}
.

The irreducible components of
L_{\xi_{1}}|_{\Theta_{*}}
are:
L_{2\sigma_{1}}|_{\Theta_{*}}
and
Q_{\xi}|_{\mathcal{K}_{1}}
. Each of them have multiplicity one when restricted to a general fiber over
\Theta_{*}
. Thus,

[L_{\xi_{1}}]\Theta_{\xi}=[L_{2\sigma_{1}}]\Theta_{\xi}+\dfrac{1}{2}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}.[Q_{\xi}]=H_{J}^{2}\Theta_{\xi}+H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}+\dfrac{1}{2}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}.(2H_{J})=H_{J}^{2}\Theta_{\xi}+2H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}\implies c=2.

For
1\leq i,j\leq 6
, let
\xi_{ij}\hookrightarrow J_{*}(3)
be the locus of points such that
\{H^{0}(\mathscr{C}_{p},(\xi(-\sigma_{i}-\sigma_{j}))|_{\mathscr{C}_{p}})>0\}_{p\in J_{*}(3)}
.
We have that
L_{\xi_{1}},L_{\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}}
coincide over the locus
\mathcal{K}_{123}
and intersect transversally along
\pi^{*}\sigma_{2},\pi^{*}\sigma_{3}
over the loci
\xi_{12},\xi_{13}
respectively. Using Theorem
4.10
, we have

\displaystyle[L_{\xi_{1}}][L_{\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}}]=L_{\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}}.\mathcal{K}_{123}+[\pi^{*}\sigma_{2}].\pi_{\xi}^{*}\xi_{12}+[\pi^{*}\sigma_{3}].\pi_{\xi}^{*}\xi_{13}=\dfrac{1}{2}L_{\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}+[K_{\pi_{J}}]_{\mathbb{P}_{\xi}}\Theta_{\xi}\implies

\displaystyle 2H_{J}^{3}\Theta_{\xi}+(2+d)H_{J}^{2}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}=2H_{J}^{3}\Theta_{\xi}+\dfrac{5}{2}H_{J}^{2}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}\implies d=\dfrac{1}{2}\implies[L_{\xi_{1}}]=H_{J}^{2}+2H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}+\dfrac{1}{2}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}.

Moving to
H_{\xi_{2}}
, put
[H_{\xi_{2}}]=H_{J}+\alpha\Theta_{\xi}
for some
\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}
.

We restrict our attention to
J_{*}(3)\setminus\mathcal{K}_{1}
.

The transverse intersection of
(H_{\xi_{2}})_{J_{*}(3)\setminus\mathcal{K}_{1}},(\pi^{*}\sigma_{3})_{J_{*}(3)\setminus\mathcal{K}_{1}}
shows that

\pi_{\xi*}([(H_{\xi_{2}})_{J_{*}(3)\setminus\mathcal{K}_{1}}\cap(\pi^{*}\sigma_{3})_{J_{*}(3)\setminus\mathcal{K}_{1}}])=\Theta_{*},

where we identify
\pi_{\xi}
with it’s restriction over
J_{*}(3)\setminus\mathcal{K}_{1}
, giving us
\alpha=1
.

Lastly, we find
[Q_{\xi}]=2H_{J}+e\Theta_{*}
for some
e\in\mathbb{Q}
.

Restricting to the open
T:=J_{*}(3)\setminus(\mathcal{K}_{1}\cup\mathcal{K}_{2})\subset J_{*}(3)
, we have

(Q_{\xi}.L_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}})_{T}=(2H_{J}+e\Theta_{*})(H_{J}^{2}+H_{J}\Theta_{*})=2H_{J}^{3}+(e+2)H_{J}^{2}\Theta_{\xi}.

The intersection
(Q_{\xi}\cap L_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}})_{T}
is transverse over
(\pi^{*}\sigma_{1})_{T},(\pi^{*}\sigma_{2})_{T},L_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}|_{\xi_{12}}
. That is,

(Q_{\xi}.L_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}})_{T}=[\pi^{*}\sigma_{1}]_{T}+[\pi^{*}\sigma_{2}]_{T}+([L_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}].\xi_{12})_{T}=2H_{J}^{3}+3H_{J}^{2}\Theta_{\xi}\implies e=1\implies[Q_{\xi}]=2H_{J}+\Theta_{\xi}.

∎

We can calculate the proper transforms of these cycles in
A^{*}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi})
. The proper transform of
Q_{\xi}
is
E_{\xi}
. Let
\tilde{L}_{\alpha},\tilde{H_{\beta}}
be the proper transforms of the loci
L_{\alpha},H_{\beta}
for
\alpha\in\{\xi_{i},2\sigma_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq 6},\beta\in\{\xi_{j}\}_{2\leq j\leq 6}
respectively.
Additionally,

(12)

[\tilde{H}_{\xi_{2}}]=\sigma_{\pi}^{*}([H_{\xi_{2}}])=\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(H_{J}+\Theta_{\xi})\in A^{*}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}).

Theorem 4.6
.

We have the following expressions for the aforementioned loci in
A^{*}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi})
:

[E_{\xi}]=\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(2H_{J}+\Theta_{\xi})-E_{\sigma},[\tilde{L}_{2\sigma_{1}}]=\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(H_{J}^{2}+H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}+\dfrac{1}{2}\Theta_{\xi}^{2})-K_{\sigma},[\tilde{L}_{\xi_{1}}]=\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(H_{J}^{2}+2H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}+\dfrac{1}{2}\Theta_{\xi}^{2})-\xi_{\sigma}-E_{\sigma}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi}.

Let
\tilde{\sigma}_{i}:=\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\pi^{*}\sigma_{i})\cap E_{\xi}\text{ for }1\leq i\leq 6
, it follows that
[\tilde{\sigma}_{i}]=\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(H_{J}^{3}+H_{J}^{2}\Theta_{\xi}+\dfrac{1}{2}H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}^{2})
.

Proof.

For a subvariety
V\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}_{\xi}
of dimension
d
, let
\tilde{V}
be its proper tranform. If
\dim(V\cap\mathscr{C}_{J})\leq d-1
, by Theorem
2.5
, we have

[\tilde{V}]=\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(V)-i_{\sigma*}(\sigma_{\pi}|_{E_{\sigma}}^{*}(V\cap\mathscr{C}_{J})_{(d-1)}).

Let
Z=Im(\overline{\Sigma})
be as defined in the proof of Theorem
4.4
, we have that

[E_{\xi}]=\sigma_{\pi}^{*}([Q_{\xi}])-E_{\sigma},[\tilde{L}_{2\sigma_{1}}]=\sigma_{\pi}^{*}([L_{2\sigma_{1}}])-K_{\sigma},[\tilde{L}_{\xi_{1}}]=\sigma_{\pi}^{*}([L_{\xi_{1}}])-i_{\sigma*}(\sigma_{\pi}|_{E_{\sigma}}^{*}(Z\cup\pi^{*}\sigma_{1})).

Applying (
10
), we have

[Z\cup\pi^{*}\sigma_{1}]=\xi+\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{*}\implies[\tilde{L}_{\xi_{1}}]=\sigma_{\pi}^{*}([L_{\xi_{1}}])-\xi_{\sigma}-E_{\sigma}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi}.

The intersection of
\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\pi^{*}\sigma_{1}),E_{\xi}
is generically transverse and the intersection maps isomorphically onto
\pi^{*}\sigma_{1}
over
J_{*}(3)\setminus\mathcal{K}_{1}
via
\sigma_{\pi}
.

Hence,
[\tilde{\sigma}_{1}]=i_{\sigma*}(\sigma^{*}\pi^{*}\sigma_{1}).E_{\xi}=\dfrac{1}{2}K_{\sigma}E_{\xi}
.
Using the expression for
E_{\xi}
and Theorem
2.3
, we have the claimed expression for
[\tilde{\sigma}_{1}]

[\tilde{\sigma}_{1}]=\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(H_{J}^{3}+H_{J}^{2}\Theta_{\xi}+\dfrac{1}{2}H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}).

The claim for
\tilde{\sigma}_{i}
for other values of
i
follows from the
S_{6}
-action on
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}
defined using the
S_{6}
-actions on
\mathscr{C}_{J},\mathbb{P}_{\xi}
.
∎

Definition 9
.

Let
L_{2\sigma_{1}}|_{\Theta},\tilde{L}_{2\sigma_{1}}|_{\Theta},\tilde{\sigma}_{1}|_{\Theta}
be the restriction of
L_{2\sigma_{1}},\tilde{L}_{2\sigma_{1}},\tilde{\sigma}_{1}
to
\Theta_{*}
respectively and

\mathbb{P}_{2\sigma_{1}}
be the proper transform of
L_{2\sigma_{1}}|_{\Theta}
under
\sigma_{\pi}
. We have

\tilde{L}_{2\sigma_{1}}|_{\Theta}=\mathbb{P}_{2\sigma_{1}}\cup\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta)

where
\Delta\hookrightarrow\mathscr{C}_{J}
is the image of
\Delta_{1}\cap(\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\sigma_{1})
by the map
\Phi_{\mathscr{C}}:\times_{M_{*}}^{3}\mathscr{C}_{*}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}_{J}
.
Let
\tilde{\sigma}:=\overline{\tilde{\sigma}_{1}|_{\Theta}\setminus\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta|_{\mathcal{K}_{1}})}
. The locus
\tilde{\sigma}_{1}|_{\Theta}
is reducible with irreducible components
\tilde{\sigma},\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta|_{\mathcal{K}_{1}})
. Hence

\tilde{\sigma}_{1}|_{\Theta}=\tilde{\sigma}\cup\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta|_{\mathcal{K}_{1}}).

Theorem 4.7
.

We have the following relations in
A^{*}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi})
:

[\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta)]=(\xi_{\sigma}-K_{\sigma})\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi}+\dfrac{1}{2}E_{\sigma}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi}^{2},\quad[\tilde{\sigma}]=\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(H_{J}^{3}\Theta_{\xi}+H_{J}^{2}\Theta_{\xi}^{2})-\dfrac{1}{4}K_{\sigma}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}.

Proof.

The restriction of
Z
to
\Theta_{*}
, namely
Z|_{\Theta_{*}}
, is reducible with irreducible components
\Delta,\pi^{*}\sigma_{1}|_{\Theta_{*}},\pi_{J}^{-1}(\mathcal{K}_{1})
.
Restriction to a general fiber over
\Theta_{*}
shows that multiplicities of
\Delta,\pi^{*}\sigma_{1}|_{\Theta_{*}}
are one. The multiplicity of
\pi_{J}^{-1}(\mathcal{K}_{1})
in
[Z|_{\Theta_{*}}]
follows from restriction to
\pi^{*}\sigma_{2}|_{\Theta_{*}}
, giving us the following in
A^{*}(\mathscr{C}_{J})

\displaystyle[Z|_{\Theta_{*}}]=[\Delta]+[\pi^{*}\sigma_{1}|_{\Theta_{*}}]+[\pi_{J}^{-1}(\mathcal{K}_{1})]\implies(\xi-\dfrac{1}{2}K_{\pi_{J}}+\Theta_{*})\Theta_{*}=[\Delta]+\dfrac{1}{2}K_{\pi_{J}}\Theta_{*}+\dfrac{1}{2}\Theta_{*}^{2}

\displaystyle\implies[\Delta]=(\xi-K_{\pi_{J}}+\dfrac{1}{2}\Theta_{*})\Theta_{*}.

This translates to the following in
A^{*}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi})

[\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta)]=i_{\sigma*}\sigma^{*}([\Delta]_{A^{*}(\mathscr{C}_{J})})=(\xi_{\sigma}-K_{\sigma})\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi}+\dfrac{1}{2}E_{\sigma}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}.

The definition of
\tilde{\sigma}
provides the following relation in
A^{*}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi})

[\tilde{\sigma}_{1}]\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi}=[\tilde{\sigma}]+[\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta)]\dfrac{1}{2}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi}\implies[\tilde{\sigma}]=\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(H_{J}^{3}\Theta_{\xi}+H_{J}^{2}\Theta_{\xi}^{2})-\dfrac{1}{4}K_{\sigma}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}.

∎

4.5.
Linear generators of
\mathbf{A_{*}(B_{*})}

We stratify
B_{*}=B_{V}\cup B_{\Theta}
to compute generators of
A^{*}(B_{*})
. The excision sequence is then

(††)

A_{*-1}(B_{\Theta})\rightarrow A_{*}(B_{*})\rightarrow A_{*}(B_{V})\rightarrow 0.

We calculate Chow groups of
B_{V},B_{\Theta}
and then use this sequence to find generators of
A_{*}(B_{*})
.

4.5.1.
\mathbf{A^{*}(B_{V})}

Let
E_{V},Q_{V},\mathbb{P}_{V},\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{V}\text{ and }\mathscr{C}_{V}\xrightarrow{\pi_{V}}V
be the restrictions of
E_{\xi},Q_{\xi},\mathbb{P}_{\xi},\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}
and the family
\pi_{J}
to
V
respectively.
Put
\mathbb{P}^{K}_{*}:=P_{V}(\pi_{V*}K_{\pi_{V}})^{\vee},\mathbb{P}^{\xi}_{*}:=P_{V}(\pi_{V*}\xi|_{\mathscr{C}_{V}})^{\vee}
. We have the following isomorphism.

Lemma 4.12
.

There is a canonical isomorphism

\pi_{V*}(K_{\pi_{V}}\otimes\xi|_{\mathscr{C}_{V}})\simeq\pi_{V*}(K_{\pi_{V}})\otimes\pi_{V*}(\xi|_{\mathscr{C}_{V}}).

Proof.

The following canonical morphisms are surjections by the cohomology and base change theorem

\pi_{V}^{*}\pi_{V*}K_{\pi_{V}}\twoheadrightarrow K_{\pi_{V}},\pi_{V}^{*}\pi_{V*}\xi|_{\mathscr{C}_{V}}\twoheadrightarrow\xi_{\mathscr{C}_{V}}.

Hence, we have the surjection
\pi_{V}^{*}(\pi_{V*}K_{\pi_{V}}\otimes\pi_{V*}\xi|_{\mathscr{C}_{V}})\twoheadrightarrow K_{\pi_{V}}\otimes\xi|_{\mathscr{C}_{V}}
. Using adjoint properties of
\pi_{V}^{*},\pi_{V*}
; we have the morphism

\pi_{V*}K_{\pi_{V}}\otimes\pi_{V*}\xi|_{\mathscr{C}_{V}}\xrightarrow{\otimes_{K\xi}}\pi_{V*}(K_{\pi_{V}}\otimes\xi|_{\mathscr{C}_{V}}).

Another application of cohomology and base change theorem shows that
(\otimes_{K\xi})_{v}
is an isomorphism for all
v\in V
.
∎

Theorem 4.8
.

The isomorphism
Q_{V}\simeq\mathbb{P}^{K}_{*}\times_{V}\mathbb{P}^{\xi}_{*}
defines rulings on
Q_{V}
and the embedding
Q_{V}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}_{V}
coincides with the Segre embedding
\mathbb{P}^{K}_{*}\times_{V}\mathbb{P}^{\xi}_{*}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}_{V}
via this isomorphism.

Proof.

We show that there is a universal analogue of the Segre embedding
\mathbb{P}^{K}_{*}\times_{V}\mathbb{P}^{\xi}_{*}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}_{V}\simeq P_{V}(\pi_{V*}(K_{\pi_{V}}\otimes\xi|_{\mathscr{C}_{V}}))^{\vee}
, as outlined in Remark
10
.

Let
\pi^{K}:\mathbb{P}^{K}_{*}\rightarrow V,\pi^{\xi}:\mathbb{P}^{\xi}_{*}\rightarrow V,\pi^{K\xi}:\mathbb{P}^{K}_{*}\times_{V}\mathbb{P}^{\xi}_{*}\rightarrow V
be the respective canonical morphisms. We have the surjections
\pi^{K*}\pi_{V*}K_{\pi_{V}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{K}_{*}}(1),\pi^{\xi*}\pi_{V*}\xi|_{\mathscr{C}_{V}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{\xi}_{*}}(1)
, inducing the surjection
\pi^{K\xi*}(\pi_{V*}K_{\pi_{V}}\otimes\pi_{V*}\xi|_{\mathscr{C}_{V}})\twoheadrightarrow\pi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{K}_{*}}(1)\otimes\pi_{2}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{\xi}_{*}}(1)
where
\pi_{1},\pi_{2}
are projections onto the first and second factors of
\mathbb{P}^{K}_{*}\times_{V}\mathbb{P}^{\xi}_{*}
.

We have the surjection
\pi^{K\xi*}\pi_{V*}(K_{\pi_{V}}\otimes\xi|_{\mathscr{C}_{V}})\twoheadrightarrow\pi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{K}_{*}}(1)\otimes\pi_{2}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{\xi}_{*}}(1)
by Lemma
4.12
, inducing the morphism
\mathbb{P}^{K}_{*}\times_{V}\mathbb{P}^{\xi}_{*}\xrightarrow{i_{K\xi}}\mathbb{P}_{V}
.
The restriction to fibers over
V
of
i_{K\xi}
is the Segre embedding by Remark
10
. Hence,
i_{K\xi}
is an embedding and its image is a family of smooth quadric surfaces parametrized by
V
.

The relative linear systems
\mathscr{C}_{V}\xrightarrow{|K_{\pi_{V}}|}\mathbb{P}^{K}_{*},\mathscr{C}_{V}\xrightarrow{|\xi_{\mathscr{C}_{V}}|}\mathbb{P}^{\xi}_{*}
induce an embedding
\mathscr{C}_{V}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{K}_{*}\times_{V}\mathbb{P}^{\xi}_{*}
. This embedding, coupled with
\mathbb{P}^{K}_{*}\times_{V}\mathbb{P}^{\xi}_{*}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}_{V}
implies that
Q_{V}\simeq\mathbb{P}^{K}_{*}\times_{J_{*}(3)}\mathbb{P}^{\xi}_{*}
since we have fiberwise equality of images over
V
by Remark
10
.
∎

Consequently, we can conclude the following about
A^{*}(E_{V}),A^{*}(B_{V})
.

Theorem 4.9
.

The restrictions of the cycles
E_{\xi},\tilde{L}_{2\sigma_{1}},\tilde{L}_{\xi_{1}},\tilde{\sigma}_{1}
to
V
linearly span
A^{*}(E_{V})
.
The restriction
(\tilde{L}_{2\sigma_{1}})_{V}
of
\tilde{L}_{2\sigma_{1}}
, to
V
, maps isomorphically onto
B_{V}
via
\Phi_{\xi}
.

Proof.

By Remark
10
,
(\tilde{L}_{2\sigma_{1}})_{V},(\tilde{L}_{\xi_{1}})_{V}
are contained in
E_{V}
and belong to the rulings
\mathbb{P}_{*}^{\xi},\mathbb{P}_{*}^{K}
respectively.

The projections
E_{V}\xrightarrow{\pi_{K}}\mathbb{P}_{*}^{K}\rightarrow V
form a tower of
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundles with sections
\gamma_{1}:V\rightarrow\mathbb{P}_{*}^{K},\gamma_{2}:\mathbb{P}_{*}^{K}\rightarrow E_{V}
given by
the images of
(\pi^{*}\sigma_{1})_{V}
under the relative linear systems
|K_{\pi_{V}}|,\mathbb{P}_{*}^{K}\times_{V}|\xi_{\mathscr{C}_{V}}|
respectively.
Hence,
A^{*}(E_{V})
is linearly spanned by

E_{V},\pi_{K}^{-1}(Im(\gamma_{1}))=\tilde{L}_{2\sigma_{1}},Im(\gamma_{2})=\tilde{L}_{\xi_{1}},\text{ and }Im(\gamma_{2})\cap\pi_{K}^{-1}(Im(\gamma_{1}))=\tilde{L}_{2\sigma_{1}}\cap\tilde{L}_{\xi_{1}}=\tilde{\sigma}_{1}

since
A^{*}(V)=\mathbb{Q}
.
By Theorem
4.2
, we have
E_{V}\simeq Q_{V}
and the fibers of the map
E_{V}\rightarrow B_{V}
are identical to the fibers of the projection
\mathbb{P}^{K}_{*}\times_{V}\mathbb{P}^{\xi}_{*}\rightarrow\mathbb{P}^{\xi}_{*}
by Remark
10
.

The sections
\sigma_{i}^{K}:V\xrightarrow{\sigma_{i}|_{V}}\mathscr{C}_{V}\xrightarrow{|K_{\pi_{V}}|}\mathbb{P}^{K}_{*}
for
1\leq i\leq 6
, induce isomorphisms
b_{i}:\mathbb{P}^{\xi}_{*}\rightarrow B_{V}
by Remark
10
and Theorem
2.1
where we identify the sections
\sigma_{i}
with their pullbacks to
J_{*}(3)
.
In fact, the images of any closed point under
\{b_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq 6}
are equal via by Theorem
4.2
and Theorem
2.6
.

This shows that the maps
b_{i}
are equal for all
1\leq i\leq 6
.
The isomorphism
b_{1}
corresponds to
\Phi_{\xi}
restricted to
(\tilde{L}_{2\sigma_{1}})_{V}
proving the last part of the theorem.
∎

Corollary 5
.

The image of
(\tilde{\sigma}_{1})_{V}
under
\Phi_{\xi}
is a section of the
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle
B_{V}
over
V
. Therefore,
A_{*}(B_{V})
is linearly spanned by the cycles
[B_{V}],[\Phi_{\xi}(\tilde{\sigma}_{1})_{V}]
.

4.5.2.
\mathbf{A^{*}(B_{\Theta})}

Let
E_{\Theta},Q_{\Theta},\mathbb{P}_{\Theta},\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\Theta}
and
\mathscr{C}_{\Theta}\xrightarrow{\pi_{\Theta}}\Theta_{*}
be the pullbacks of
E_{\xi},Q_{\xi},\mathbb{P}_{\xi},\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}
and the restriction of the family
\pi_{J}
, respectively, over
\Theta_{*}\hookrightarrow J_{*}(3)
.

The locus
Q_{\Theta}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}_{\Theta}
is a family of quadric cones with vertices parameterized by
\Delta
by Theorem
4.2
. Let
E_{\Delta}:=\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta)
be the exceptional locus over
\Delta\hookrightarrow\mathscr{C}_{J}
.

The restriction of
\Phi_{\xi}
to
E_{\Delta}
is an isomorphism and
Q_{\Theta}\setminus\Delta\simeq E_{\Theta}\setminus E_{\Delta}
by Lemma
4.3
.

Lemma 4.13
.

The blowup
bl_{\Delta}Q_{\Theta}
embeds into
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\Theta}
with image
E_{\Theta}
.

Proof.

We have
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\Theta}=bl_{\mathscr{C}_{\Theta}}\mathbb{P}_{\Theta}
since
\mathscr{C}_{J},Q_{\xi}
are flat over
J_{*}(3)
. Therefore, we have the embedding
bl_{\mathscr{C}_{\Theta}}Q_{\Theta}\hookrightarrow\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\Theta}
and
E_{\Theta}=bl_{\mathscr{C}_{\Theta}}Q_{\Theta}
by Remark
11
.

We have an induced morphism
E_{\Theta}\rightarrow bl_{\Delta}Q_{\Theta}
which induces a bijection of closed points on each fiber over
\Theta_{*}
.
Therefore, we have
E_{\Theta}=bl_{\mathscr{C}_{\Theta}}Q_{\Theta}\simeq bl_{\Delta}Q_{\Theta}
.
∎

Additionally,
\Delta
is a Cartier divisor on
\mathscr{C}_{\Theta}
and
\mathscr{C}_{\Theta}\hookrightarrow Q_{\Theta}
. Therefore, we have an induced embedding
i_{\mathscr{C}}:\mathscr{C}_{\Theta}\simeq bl_{\Delta}\mathscr{C}_{\Theta}\hookrightarrow bl_{\Delta}Q_{\Theta}=E_{\Theta}
.

Remark 16
.

By Lemma
4.3
, we have
\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta)
maps isomorphically onto
B_{\Theta}
via
\Phi_{\xi}
.

Let
Y:=P_{\Theta_{*}}(\pi_{\Theta*}K_{\pi_{\Theta}})^{\vee}\xrightarrow{p_{Y}}\Theta_{*}
be the
g^{1}_{2}
space for
\mathscr{C}_{\Theta}\xrightarrow{\pi_{\Theta}}\Theta_{*}
. Following a similar line of reasoning as in Remark
9
, the composition
\phi_{\xi}:\mathscr{C}_{\Theta}\xrightarrow{i_{\mathscr{C}}}E_{\Theta}\xrightarrow{\Phi_{\xi}}B_{\Theta}
is invariant under the hyperelliptic involution, namely
h_{\Theta}
, of
\mathscr{C}_{\Theta}
. Hence,

\phi_{\xi}
factors through
\tilde{\phi}_{\xi}:Y\xrightarrow{\sim}B_{\Theta}
and is an isomorphism by Theorem
2.1
.

Let
\delta\in H^{1}(\Theta_{*},\mathcal{L}_{\Delta}^{\vee}\otimes\pi_{\Theta*}K_{\pi_{\Theta}}^{2})
be the extension class of the following exact sequence on
\Theta_{*}

0\rightarrow\pi_{\Theta*}K_{\pi_{\Theta}}^{2}\rightarrow\pi_{\Theta*}(K_{\pi_{\Theta}}^{2}(\Delta))\rightarrow{\mathcal{L}_{\Delta}}\rightarrow 0

where
\mathcal{L}_{\Delta}:=\pi_{\Theta*}(\mathcal{O}_{\Delta}(K_{\pi_{\Theta}}^{2}(\Delta)))
. The sequence of morphisms

H^{1}(\Theta_{*},\mathcal{L}_{\Delta}^{\vee}\otimes\pi_{\Theta*}K_{\pi_{\Theta}}^{2})\xrightarrow{\pi_{Y}^{*}}H^{1}(Y,\pi_{Y}^{*}\pi_{\Theta*}K_{\pi_{\Theta}}^{2}\otimes\pi_{Y}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\Delta}^{\vee})\simeq H^{1}(Y,\pi_{Y}^{*}\pi_{Y*}\mathcal{O}_{Y}(2)\otimes\pi_{Y}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\Delta}^{\vee})\rightarrow H^{1}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y}(2)\otimes\pi_{Y}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\Delta}^{\vee})

maps
\delta
to the class of an extension

0\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{Y}(2)\rightarrow\mathscr{E}\rightarrow\pi_{Y}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\Delta}\rightarrow 0

on
Y
. Let
\mathbb{P}_{Y}:=P_{Y}(\mathscr{E}^{\vee})\xrightarrow{p_{\mathscr{E}}}Y
.
The surjection
\mathscr{E}\twoheadrightarrow\pi_{Y}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\Delta}
defines a section
s:Y\rightarrow\mathbb{P}_{Y}
.

Theorem 4.10
.

We have
\mathbb{P}_{Y}\simeq E_{\Theta}
and the induced morphism
\mathbb{P}_{Y}\rightarrow Q_{\Theta}
maps the section
s:Y\rightarrow\mathbb{P}_{Y}
to the section
\Delta:\Theta_{*}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}_{\Theta}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}_{\Theta}
.

Proof.

In order to define the isomorphism between
\mathbb{P}_{Y},E_{\Theta}
, we construct a blow-down morphism
\mathbb{P}_{Y}\rightarrow\mathbb{P}_{\Theta}
.
The extension class of
\mathscr{E}
induces the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{Y}^{*}\pi_{Y*}\mathcal{O}_{Y}(2)\simeq\pi_{Y}^{*}\pi_{\Theta*}K_{\pi_{\Theta}}^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{Y}^{*}\pi_{\Theta*}K_{\pi_{\Theta}}^{2}(\Delta)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{Y}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{Y}(2)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{Y}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}

The left and right vertical arrows are surjective. By the snake lemma, the induced map
\pi_{Y}^{*}\pi_{\Theta*}(K_{\pi_{\Theta}}^{2}(\Delta))\rightarrow\mathscr{E}
is a surjection. This induces an embedding
i_{Y}:\mathbb{P}_{Y}\hookrightarrow Y\times_{\Theta_{*}}\mathbb{P}_{\Delta}
.

We shall show that the restricted projection
\mathbb{P}_{Y}\xrightarrow{\pi_{\mathscr{E}}}\mathbb{P}_{\Delta}\simeq\mathbb{P}_{\Theta}
is the desired blow-down morphism mapping onto
Q_{\Theta}
. The isomorphism between
\mathbb{P}_{Y}
and
E_{\Theta}
would then follow from the universal property of blowups.

Next, we show that the embedding
\mathscr{C}_{\Theta}\xrightarrow{j|_{\mathscr{C}_{\Theta}}}\mathbb{P}_{\Theta}
factors through
\pi_{\mathscr{E}}
.

Let
\mathscr{E}^{\prime}:=|K_{\pi_{\Theta}}|^{*}(\mathscr{E})
be the pullback of
\mathscr{E}
to
\mathscr{C}_{\Theta}
via the
g^{1}_{2}
map
|K_{\pi_{\Theta}}|:\mathscr{C}_{\Theta}\rightarrow Y
and, for any sheaf
G
on
\mathscr{C}_{\Theta}
, put
\mathcal{F}_{G}:=ker(\pi_{\Theta}^{*}\pi_{\Theta*}G\rightarrow G)
.

The following diagram shows that the surjection
\pi_{\Theta}^{*}\pi_{\Theta*}K_{\pi_{\Theta}}^{2}(\Delta)\twoheadrightarrow K_{\pi_{\Theta}}^{2}(\Delta)
factors via
\pi_{\Theta}^{*}\pi_{\Theta*}K_{\pi_{\Theta}}^{2}(\Delta)\twoheadrightarrow\mathscr{E}^{\prime}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}_{K_{\pi_{\Theta}}^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}_{K_{\pi_{\Theta}}^{2}(\Delta)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\Theta}^{*}\pi_{\Theta*}K_{\pi_{\Theta}}^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\Theta}^{*}\pi_{\Theta*}K_{\pi_{\Theta}}^{2}(\Delta)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\Theta}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{\pi_{\Theta}}^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{E}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\Theta}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{\pi_{\Theta}}^{2}(\Delta)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{\pi_{\Theta}}^{2}(\Delta)|_{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}

A straightforward diagram chase shows that
ker(\pi_{\Theta}^{*}\pi_{\Theta*}K_{\pi_{\Theta}}^{2}(\Delta)\rightarrow\mathscr{E}^{\prime})=\mathcal{F}_{K_{\pi_{\Theta}}^{2}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{K_{\pi_{\Theta}}^{2}(\Delta)}
and the surjection
\pi_{\Theta}^{*}\pi_{\Theta*}K_{\pi_{\Theta}}^{2}(\Delta)\twoheadrightarrow K_{\pi_{\Theta}}^{2}(\Delta)
factors via
\mathscr{E}^{\prime}
, giving us the following diagram

(13)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}_{Y}\simeq E_{\Theta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{C}_{\Theta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}_{\Theta}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathscr{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\mathscr{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces|K_{\pi_{\Theta}}|}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j|_{\mathscr{C}_{\Theta}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}

The embedding
i_{Y}
shows that fibers of
\mathbb{P}_{Y}
over
Y
map to lines in
\mathbb{P}_{\Delta}
via
\pi_{\mathscr{E}}
. For
y\in Y
, the image of
p_{\mathscr{E}}^{-1}(y)
embeds in
\mathbb{P}_{\Theta}
as a line via
\pi_{\mathscr{E}}
, joining the two(possibly degenerate) points in
|K_{\pi_{\Theta}}|^{-1}(y)
.

Additionally, the image of the section
s
over
Y
via
\pi_{\mathscr{E}}
is the section
\Delta
of
\mathbb{P}_{\Theta}
over
\Theta_{*}
, where we identify
\Delta
with its image under
j
.

Therefore,
\mathbb{P}_{Y}
maps onto
Q_{\Theta}
via
\pi_{\mathscr{E}}
using Theorem
4.2
. The universal property of blowups and Remark
10
then imply that
\mathbb{P}_{Y}\simeq E_{\Theta}
as claimed.
∎

Corollary 6
.

The composition
\Phi_{\xi}\circ s:Y\rightarrow\mathbb{P}_{Y}\simeq E_{\Theta}\rightarrow B_{\Theta}
maps
Y
isomorphically onto
B_{\Theta}
by Remark
16
.

Theorem 4.11
.

For any
\alpha\in A^{*}(E_{\Theta})
, put
\alpha|_{\mathcal{K}_{1}}:=(\sigma_{\pi}\circ\pi_{\xi})^{-1}(\mathcal{K}_{1})\cap\alpha
and
E_{\mathcal{K}_{1}}:=E_{\Theta}|_{\mathcal{K}_{1}}
.
The following cycles linearly span
A^{*}(E_{\Theta})

E_{\Theta},E_{\mathcal{K}_{1}},\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta),\mathbb{P}_{2\sigma_{1}},\mathbb{P}_{2\sigma_{1}}|_{\mathcal{K}_{1}},\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta)|_{\mathcal{K}_{1}},\tilde{\sigma},\tilde{\sigma}|_{\mathcal{K}_{1}}.

Proof.

The tower of
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundles
\mathbb{P}_{Y}\xrightarrow{p_{\mathcal{E}}}Y\xrightarrow{p_{Y}}\Theta_{*}
with
Y\xrightarrow[\simeq]{p_{Y}}\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\Theta_{*}
shows that

A^{*}(E_{\Theta})=p_{\mathcal{E}}^{*}(A^{*}(Y))\oplus\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta).p_{\mathcal{E}}^{*}(A^{*}(Y)),A^{*}(Y)\simeq\mathbb{Q}[\zeta_{Y},p_{Y}^{*}(\mathcal{K}_{1})]/(p_{Y}^{*}(\mathcal{K}_{1})^{2},\zeta_{Y}^{2})

where
\zeta_{Y}
is the relative hyperplane class of
Y
over
\Theta_{*}
. Put
\Gamma:=|K_{\pi_{\Theta}}|(\sigma_{1})
. We have that
A^{*}(Y)
is linearly spanned by

[Y],[\Gamma],[p_{Y}^{*}(\mathcal{K}_{1})],[p_{Y}^{*}(\mathcal{K}_{1})\cap\Gamma]

since
\Gamma
is a section of
p_{Y}
. The locus
\mathbb{P}_{2\sigma_{1}}
coincides with the pullback
p_{\mathcal{E}}^{*}(\Gamma)
, which intersects
\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta)
transversally.
Thus,
A^{*}(E_{\Theta})
is linearly spanned by

E_{\Theta},E_{\mathcal{K}_{1}},\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta),\mathbb{P}_{2\sigma_{1}},\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{K}_{1}},\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta)|_{\mathcal{K}_{1}},\mathbb{P}_{2\sigma_{1}}\cap\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta),(\mathbb{P}_{2\sigma_{1}}\cap\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta))|_{\mathcal{K}_{1}}

as claimed, since
\mathbb{P}_{2\sigma_{1}},\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta),(\mathbb{P}_{2\sigma_{1}}\cap\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta))
are flat over
\Theta_{*}
.
Additionally,
\tilde{\sigma},\mathbb{P}_{2\sigma_{1}}\cap\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta)
map isomorphically onto
Y
via
p_{\mathscr{E}}
.
Thus,

<\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta)|_{\mathcal{K}_{1}},\mathbb{P}_{2\sigma_{1}}\cap\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta)>=<\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta)|_{\mathcal{K}_{1}},\tilde{\sigma}>,\quad[(\mathbb{P}_{2\sigma_{1}}\cap\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta))|_{\mathcal{K}_{1}}]=[\tilde{\sigma}|_{\mathcal{K}_{1}}].

∎

Corollary 7
.

Let
B_{\mathcal{K}_{1}}
be the restriction of
B_{*}
over
\mathcal{K}_{1}\hookrightarrow J_{*}(3)
. The following cycles linearly span
A^{*}(B_{\Theta})

\{[B_{\Theta}],[B_{\mathcal{K}_{1}}],[\Phi_{\xi}(\tilde{\sigma}_{1})|_{\Theta_{*}}],[\Phi_{\xi}(\tilde{\sigma}_{1})|_{\mathcal{K}_{2}}]\}.

Proof.

Using Theorem
4.10
, we have that
A^{*}(\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta))\simeq A^{*}(Y)
is spanned by

\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta),\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta)|_{\mathcal{K}_{1}},\mathbb{P}_{2\sigma_{1}}\cap\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta),(\mathbb{P}_{2\sigma_{1}}\cap\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta))|_{\mathcal{K}_{1}}.

The images of these cycles under
\Phi_{\xi}
linearly span
A^{*}(B_{\Theta})
by Corollary
6
.

We have
\Phi_{\xi}(\tilde{\sigma})_{p}=\Phi_{\xi}(\mathbb{P}_{2\sigma_{1}})_{p}=\Phi_{\xi}(\mathbb{P}_{2\sigma_{1}}\cap\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta))_{p}\forall p\in\Theta_{*}
by Remark
8
. Hence, we have

\Phi_{\xi}(\mathbb{P}_{2\sigma_{1}}\cap\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta))=\Phi_{\xi}(\tilde{\sigma}),\Phi_{\xi}(\mathbb{P}_{2\sigma_{1}}\cap\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta))|_{\mathcal{K}_{1}}=\Phi_{\xi}(\tilde{\sigma})|_{\mathcal{K}_{1}}.

On the other hand,
\Phi_{\xi}(\tilde{\sigma}_{1})|_{\Theta_{*}}
is reducible with irreducible components
\Phi_{\xi}(\tilde{\sigma}),B_{\mathcal{K}_{1}}
, each with multiplicity one on a general fiber over
\Theta_{*}
. Thus,

[\Phi_{\xi}(\tilde{\sigma}_{1})|_{\Theta_{*}}]=[\Phi_{\xi}(\tilde{\sigma})]+[B_{\mathcal{K}_{1}}]

in
A^{*}(B_{\Theta})
and

[\Phi_{\xi}(\tilde{\sigma})|_{\mathcal{K}_{1}}]=[\Phi_{\xi}(\tilde{\sigma})].\dfrac{1}{2}det_{\xi}^{*}\Theta_{3}=[\Phi_{\xi}(\tilde{\sigma}_{1})|_{\Theta_{*}}].\dfrac{1}{2}det_{\xi}^{*}\Theta_{3}=[\Phi_{\xi}(\tilde{\sigma}_{1})|_{\mathcal{K}_{2}}].

∎

4.5.3.
First excision

We use results from §
4.5.1
, §
4.5.2
; specifically Corollary
5
, Corollary
7
, and the excision sequence (
††
‣
4.5
).
Combining Corollary
4.5.1
, and Corollary
4.5.2
; we get the following theorem.

Theorem 4.12
.

The following cycles linearly span
A_{*}(B_{*})
:

[B_{*}],[B_{*}|_{\Theta_{*}}],[\Phi_{\xi}(\tilde{\sigma_{1}})],[B_{*}|_{\mathcal{K}_{1}}],[\Phi_{\xi}(\tilde{\sigma_{1}})|_{\Theta_{*}}],[\Phi_{\xi}(\tilde{\sigma}_{1})|_{\mathcal{K}_{2}}].

We shall prove the linear independence of these cycles and find polynomial relations in §
4.6
.

4.6.
Chow rings of
\tilde{U}(2,3,2),U(2,3,2)

As outlined in (
†
‣
4.2
), we apply the second excision and Lemma
4.6
to get

(‡)

A^{i-2}(B_{*})\xrightarrow{i_{B*}}S^{i}(\tilde{U}(2,3,2))\rightarrow S^{i}(\tilde{U}(2,3,2)\setminus B_{*})\rightarrow 0\quad\forall i\geq 0.

We have
\tilde{U}(2,3,2)\setminus B_{*}\simeq\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}\setminus E_{\xi}
. To calculate
S^{*}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}\setminus E_{\xi})
, we use the excision sequence

S^{*}(E_{\xi})\rightarrow S^{*}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi})\rightarrow S^{*}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}\setminus E_{\xi})\rightarrow 0.

Lemma 4.14
.

Excision on
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}
yields

S^{*}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}\setminus E_{\xi})\simeq\mathbb{Q}[\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J},\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi}]/(\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(H_{J}^{3}+H_{J}^{2}\Theta_{\xi}+\dfrac{1}{2}H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}),\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(H_{J}^{2}\Theta_{\xi}^{2})).

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{I}:=i_{\xi*}(A^{*}(E_{\xi}))
be the ideal generated by pushing forward the cycles in
A^{*}(E_{\xi})
to
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}
via the inclusion
i_{\xi}:E_{\xi}\hookrightarrow\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}
. It follows from (
‡
‣
4.6
) that
A^{*}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}\setminus E_{\xi})\simeq A^{*}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi})/\mathcal{I}
.

The ideal
\mathcal{I}
is generated by

[E_{\xi}],[\tilde{L}_{2\sigma_{1}}],[\tilde{L}_{\xi_{1}}],[\tilde{\sigma}_{1}],[\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta)]

in
A^{*}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi})
by Theorem
4.9
and Theorem
4.11
.

Thus, we get the additional relations

E_{\sigma}-\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(2H_{J}+\Theta_{\xi})=0,\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(H_{J}^{2}-\dfrac{1}{2}\Theta_{\xi}^{2})-\xi_{\sigma}=\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(H_{J}^{2}+H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}+\dfrac{1}{2}\Theta_{\xi}^{2})-K_{\sigma}=0

using Theorem
4.7
and Theorem
4.6
.
∎

Following Theorem
2.3
, Lemma
4.6
, Remark
13
and (
9
), we have

(14)

S^{i}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi})\simeq S^{i}(\tilde{U}(2,3,2))\oplus A^{i-1}(B_{*})\forall i\geq 1.

Theorem
4.12
and (
‡
‣
4.6
) give us the following upper bounds:

(1)

\dim S^{1}(\tilde{U}(2,3,2))=2,\dim A^{0}(B_{*})=1

(2)

\dim S^{2}(\tilde{U}(2,3,2))\leq 4,\dim A^{1}(B_{*})\leq 2

(3)

\dim S^{3}(\tilde{U}(2,3,2))\leq 4,\dim A^{2}(B_{*})\leq 2

(4)

\dim S^{4}(\tilde{U}(2,3,2))\leq 2,\dim A^{3}(B_{*})\leq 1

(5)

\dim S^{5}(\tilde{U}(2,3,2))\leq 1,\dim A^{4}(B_{*})=0

For any
1\leq j\leq 5
, we have equality in bound
(j)
for
\dim S^{j}(\tilde{U}(2,3,2))
if and only if (
‡
‣
4.6
) is left exact for codimension
j
and the generators of
A^{j-2}(B_{*})
in Theorem
4.12
are linearly independent.

We have the following dimensions of
S^{i}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi})
from Theorem
4
:

(1)

\dim S^{1}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi})=3

(2)

\dim S^{2}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi})=6

(3)

\dim S^{3}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi})=6

(4)

\dim S^{4}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi})=3

(5)

\dim S^{5}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi})=1.

Applying (
14
) to the above dimensions, we get the following Corollary.

Corollary 8
.

The aforementioned upper bounds on
\{\dim S^{i}(\tilde{U}(2,3,2))\}_{1\leq i\leq 5}
are thus in fact equalities for all
1\leq i\leq 5
, implying:

•

The linear generators for
A^{*}(B_{*})
, as described in Theorem
4.12
, are linearly independent.

•

The pushforward map in (
‡
‣
4.6
) is injective for all values of
i
.

Let
\Theta_{U}:=det_{\xi}^{*}\Theta_{3}=\Phi_{\xi*}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi},H_{U}:=\Phi_{\xi*}(\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J})\in A^{1}(\tilde{U}(2,3,2))
.
By Lemma
4.14
,
\Theta_{U},H_{U}
form a basis of
A^{1}(\tilde{U}(2,3,2))
.
The relations

\Phi_{\xi}^{*}\Theta_{U}=\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi},\Theta_{U}^{3}=0

follow from the injectivity of
\Phi_{\xi}^{*}
and shall prove useful in computations to follow.

Lemma 4.15
.

Using
[
4
, Proposition 0.1.3]
and Theorem
4.6
, we have

\displaystyle\Phi_{\xi}^{*}(H_{U})=\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(3H_{J}+\Theta_{\xi})-E_{\sigma},\Phi_{\xi*}(E_{\sigma})=2H_{U}+\Theta_{U}

\displaystyle\Phi_{\xi*}(\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}^{2})+H_{U}\Theta_{U}+\dfrac{1}{2}\Theta_{U}^{2}-\Phi_{\xi*}(K_{\sigma})=B_{*},\Phi_{\xi*}(\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}^{2})=\Phi_{\xi*}(\xi_{\sigma})+\dfrac{1}{2}\Theta_{U}^{2}.

Proof.

Consider
i_{\xi}^{*}[\tilde{H}_{\xi_{2}}]\in A^{1}(E_{\xi})=<[\tilde{L}_{\xi_{1}}],[\tilde{L}_{2\sigma_{1}}],[E_{\Theta}]>
.
Restricting to a fiber over
V\subset J_{*}(3)
, we have

(15)

i_{\xi}^{*}[\tilde{H}_{\xi_{2}}]=[\tilde{L}_{\xi_{1}}]+[\tilde{L}_{2\sigma_{1}}]+t[E_{\Theta}]

for some
t\in\mathbb{Q}
. Since
\tilde{H}_{\xi_{2}}\cap E_{\xi}
has
\tilde{L}_{2\sigma_{2}},L_{\xi_{1}}
as its irreducible components, we have
t=0
.

Hence,
\Phi_{E*}(i_{\xi}^{*}[\tilde{H}_{\xi_{2}}])=B_{*}
since
\Phi_{\xi*}([\tilde{L}_{2\sigma_{2}}])=[B_{*}],\Phi_{\xi*}([\tilde{L}_{\xi_{1}}])=0
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle[\tilde{H}_{\xi_{2}}]=\Phi_{\xi}^{*}(H_{U}+\Theta_{U})-E_{\xi}\implies\Phi_{\xi}^{*}(H_{U})=\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(3H_{J}+\Theta_{\xi})-E_{\sigma}

\displaystyle\Phi_{\xi*}(E_{\xi})=0\implies\Phi_{\xi*}(E_{\sigma})=2H_{U}+\Theta_{U}

\displaystyle\Phi_{\xi*}(\tilde{L}_{2\sigma_{1}})=B_{*}\implies\Phi_{\xi*}(\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}^{2})+H_{U}\Theta_{U}+\dfrac{1}{2}\Theta_{U}^{2}-\Phi_{\xi*}(K_{\sigma})=B_{*}

\displaystyle\Phi_{\xi*}(\tilde{L}_{\xi_{1}})=0\implies\Phi_{\xi*}(\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}^{2})=\Phi_{\xi*}(\xi_{\sigma})+\dfrac{1}{2}\Theta_{U}^{2}.

∎

Remark 17
.

The expression for
\Phi_{\xi}^{*}(H_{U})
can also be derived using
[
5
, Proposition 4.7]
.

Lemma 4.16
.

The pushforwards of
B_{*},\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}^{2},\xi_{\sigma},K_{\sigma}
under
\Phi_{\xi}
are given by the following relations:

\displaystyle\Phi_{\xi}^{*}B_{*}=\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(H_{J}^{2}+H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}+\dfrac{1}{2}\Theta_{\xi}^{2})-K_{\sigma},\Phi_{\xi*}(\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}^{2})=H_{U}^{2}-B_{*},\Phi_{\xi*}(\xi_{\sigma})=H_{U}^{2}-\dfrac{1}{2}\Theta_{U}^{2}-B_{*},

\displaystyle\Phi_{\xi*}(K_{\sigma})=H_{U}^{2}+H_{U}\Theta_{U}+\dfrac{1}{2}\Theta_{U}^{2}-2B_{*}.

Proof.

Using Lemma
4.14
, there exists
m\in\mathbb{Q}
such that
\Phi_{\xi*}(\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}^{2})=H_{U}^{2}+mB_{*}
. Hence, we have

\Phi_{\xi*}(K_{\sigma})=H_{U}^{2}+H_{U}\Theta_{U}+\dfrac{1}{2}\Theta_{U}^{2}+(m-1)B_{*},\Phi_{\xi*}(\xi_{\sigma})=H_{U}^{2}-\dfrac{1}{2}\Theta_{U}^{2}+mB_{*}.

We show that
m=-1
.
Applying
[
4
, Theorem 0.1.3]
, we get

K_{\sigma}+i_{\xi*}\Phi_{E}^{*}\Phi_{E*}i_{\xi}^{*}K_{\sigma}=\Phi_{\xi}^{*}\Phi_{\xi*}(K_{\sigma})=4\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}^{2}+6\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(H_{J}\Theta_{\xi})-2E_{\sigma}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi}-K_{\sigma}-2\xi_{\sigma}+2\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}+(m-1)\Phi_{\xi}^{*}B_{*}

using
\Phi_{E}^{*}(\Phi_{\xi}(\tilde{\sigma}_{1}))=\tilde{L}_{\xi_{1}}
, and that
\tilde{\sigma}_{1}
maps isomorphically into
B_{*}
via
\Phi_{\xi}
. Thus, we have

i_{\xi*}\Phi_{E}^{*}\Phi_{E*}i_{\xi}^{*}K_{\sigma}=2i_{\xi*}\Phi_{E}^{*}\Phi_{E*}i_{\xi}^{*}\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\pi^{*}\sigma_{1})=2i_{\xi*}\Phi_{E}^{*}\Phi_{E*}\tilde{\sigma}_{1}=2i_{\xi*}\Phi_{E}^{*}[\Phi_{\xi}(\tilde{\sigma}_{1})]=2[\tilde{L}_{\xi_{1}}].

Plugging this into the equation above and using Theorem
4.6
, we get

(16)

(m-1)B_{*}=2K_{\sigma}-2\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}^{2}-2\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(H_{J}\Theta_{\xi})-\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}.

Applying similar computations to
\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}^{2}
, we get

(17)

\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}^{2}=(\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(3H_{J}+\Theta_{\xi})-E_{\sigma})^{2}+m\Phi_{\xi}^{*}B_{*}-i_{\xi*}\Phi_{E}^{*}\Phi_{E*}i_{\xi}^{*}H_{J}^{2}.

Using
i_{\xi}^{*}H_{\xi_{2}}=\tilde{L}_{2\sigma_{2}}+\tilde{L}_{\xi_{1}}
, as proven in (
15
), we have

i_{\xi}^{*}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}=\tilde{L}_{2\sigma_{2}}+\tilde{L}_{\xi_{1}}-E_{\Theta}=\tilde{L}_{2\sigma_{3}}+\tilde{L}_{\xi_{4}}-E_{\Theta}

in
A^{*}(E_{\xi})
. Considering different loci that have generically transverse intersections in
E_{\xi}
, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{L}_{2\sigma_{i}}\tilde{L}_{2\sigma_{j}}=\sigma_{\pi}^{-1}(\Delta)\implies\Phi_{E*}(\tilde{L}_{2\sigma_{i}}\tilde{L}_{2\sigma_{j}})=B_{\Theta},\tilde{L}_{\xi_{i}}\tilde{L}_{2\sigma_{j}}=\tilde{\sigma}_{i}\implies\Phi_{E*}(\tilde{L}_{\xi_{i}}\tilde{L}_{2\sigma_{j}})=\Phi_{\xi}(\tilde{\sigma}_{i}),

\displaystyle\Phi_{E*}(\tilde{L}_{\xi_{i}}\tilde{L}_{\xi_{j}})=0

in
A^{*}(E_{\xi}),A^{*}(B_{*})
for all
1\leq i<j\leq 6
. Using
\Phi_{E}^{*}(\Phi_{\xi}(\tilde{\sigma}_{1}))=\tilde{L}_{\xi_{1}}
and
[\tilde{\sigma}_{i}]=[\tilde{\sigma}_{1}]\forall 1\leq i\leq 6
in
A^{*}(E_{\xi})
, we have the following equation in
A^{*}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi})

\displaystyle i_{\xi*}\Phi_{E}^{*}\Phi_{E*}i_{\xi}^{*}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}^{2}=i_{\xi*}\Phi_{E}^{*}(2\Phi_{\xi}(\tilde{\sigma}_{1}-B_{\Theta}))=2[\tilde{L}_{\xi_{1}}]-[E_{\Theta}]=

\displaystyle 2[\tilde{L}_{\xi_{1}}]-E_{\xi}.\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi}=2\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}^{2}-E_{\sigma}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi}+2\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(H_{J}\Theta_{\xi})-2\xi_{\sigma}.

Plugging this into (
17
), we get

(18)

m\Phi_{\xi}^{*}B_{*}=K_{\sigma}-\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}^{2}-\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(H_{J}\Theta_{\xi})-\dfrac{1}{2}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}.

Putting the equations (
16
), (
18
) together, we have
m=-1
as claimed.
∎

Theorem 4.13
.

The ring
S^{*}(\tilde{U}(2,3,2))
is generated by the cycles
H_{U},\Theta_{U},B_{*}
. In particular,

\displaystyle S^{*}(\tilde{U}(2,3,2))=\mathbb{Q}[H_{U},\Theta_{U},B_{*}]/(\Theta_{U}^{3},H_{U}^{2}\Theta_{U}^{2}-4B_{*}\Theta_{U}^{2},B_{*}H_{U}^{2}-B_{*}H_{U}\Theta_{U}+\dfrac{1}{2}B_{*}\Theta_{U}^{2},
