\displaystyle B_{*}H_{U}^{2}-B_{*}^{2},H_{U}^{3}+H_{U}^{2}\Theta_{U}+\dfrac{1}{2}H_{U}\Theta_{U}^{2}-4B_{*}H_{U}).

Proof.

We use injectivity of
\Phi_{\xi}^{*}
to compute relations in
A^{*}(\tilde{U}(2,3,2))
.

We have
\Phi_{\xi}^{*}(B_{*})=\tilde{L}_{2\sigma_{1}},[\tilde{\sigma}_{1}]=\tilde{L}_{2\sigma_{1}}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}
in
A^{*}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi})
using Theorem
4.6
. Therefore,

[\Phi_{\xi}(\tilde{\sigma}_{1})]=\Phi_{\xi*}(\tilde{\sigma}_{1})=\Phi_{\xi*}(\tilde{L}_{2\sigma_{1}}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J})=\Phi_{\xi*}(\Phi_{\xi}^{*}B_{*}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J})=B_{*}H_{U}.

Applying Theorem
4.12
, Corollary
8
and Theorem
4.14
, we have the following
\mathbb{Q}
-basis of
S^{*}(\tilde{U}(2,3,2))

\{[\tilde{U}(2,3,2)],H_{U},\Theta_{U},H_{U}^{2},H_{U}\Theta_{U},\Theta_{U}^{2},B_{*},H_{U}^{2}\Theta_{U},H_{U}\Theta_{U}^{2},B_{*}H_{U},B_{*}\Theta_{U},B_{*}H_{U}\Theta_{U},B_{*}\Theta_{U}^{2},B_{*}H_{U}\Theta_{U}^{2}\}.

Hence, it is enough to express
H_{U}^{3},B_{*}H_{U}^{2},H_{U}^{2}\Theta_{U}^{2},H_{U}^{3}\Theta_{U},B_{*}^{2}
as linear combinations of the basis elements above to describe the ring
S^{*}(\tilde{U}(2,3,2))
.
Using (
4.16
), we have

\tilde{L}_{2\sigma_{1}}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}^{2}=0\implies\Phi_{\xi*}(\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}^{2}.\Phi_{\xi}^{*}B_{*})=0\implies B_{*}\Phi_{\xi*}(\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}^{2})=0\implies B_{*}H_{U}^{2}=B_{*}^{2}

in
A^{*}(\tilde{U}(2,3,2))
. Furthermore,

B_{*}H_{U}^{2}=[\Phi_{\xi}(\tilde{\sigma}_{1})]H_{U}=\Phi_{\xi*}(\tilde{\sigma}_{1}.\Phi_{\xi}^{*}H_{U}).

On the other hand, we have the following

\displaystyle\Phi_{\xi*}(\tilde{\sigma}_{1}.\Phi_{\xi}^{*}H_{U})=\Phi_{\xi*}(\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(H_{J}^{3}+H_{J}^{2}\Theta_{\xi}+\dfrac{1}{2}H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}^{2})(\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(3H_{J}+\Theta_{\xi})-E_{\sigma}))=

\displaystyle\Phi_{\xi*}(\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(H_{J}^{3}\Theta_{\xi}+H_{J}^{2}\Theta_{\xi}^{2})+\dfrac{1}{2}(K_{\sigma}-\xi_{\sigma})\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}),

\displaystyle\Phi_{\xi*}(\tilde{\sigma}_{1})=B_{*}H_{U}=\Phi_{\xi*}(\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(H_{J}^{3}+H_{J}^{2}\Theta_{\xi}+\dfrac{1}{2}H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}^{2}))\implies\Phi_{\xi*}(\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(H_{J}^{3}))=B_{*}(H_{U}+\Theta_{U})-H_{U}^{2}\Theta_{U}-\dfrac{1}{2}H_{U}\Theta_{U}^{2}

\displaystyle\implies B_{*}H_{U}^{2}=\Phi_{\xi*}(\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}^{3})\Theta_{U}+\Phi_{\xi*}(\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}^{2})\Theta_{U}^{2}+\dfrac{1}{2}\Phi_{\xi*}(K_{\sigma}-\xi_{\sigma})\Theta_{U}^{2}=B_{*}H_{U}\Theta_{U}-\dfrac{1}{2}B_{*}\Theta_{U}^{2}.

A straightforward computation in
A^{*}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi})
shows that

\displaystyle\Phi_{\xi}^{*}(H_{U}^{3}+H_{U}^{2}\Theta_{U}+\dfrac{1}{2}H_{U}\Theta_{U}^{2})=4\Phi_{\xi}^{*}(B_{*}H_{U})\implies H_{U}^{3}+H_{U}^{2}\Theta_{U}+\dfrac{1}{2}H_{U}\Theta_{U}^{2}=4B_{*}H_{U}\text{ in }A^{*}(\tilde{U}(2,3,2)),

\displaystyle\Phi_{\xi}^{*}(4B_{*}\Theta_{U}^{2})=4(\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(H_{J}^{2}\Theta_{\xi}^{2})-K_{\sigma}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi}^{2})=\Phi_{\xi}^{*}(H_{U}^{2}\Theta_{U}^{2})\implies H_{U}^{2}\Theta_{U}^{2}=4B_{*}\Theta_{U}^{2}\text{ in }A^{*}(\tilde{U}(2,3,2)),

\displaystyle\implies H_{U}^{3}\Theta_{U}+H_{U}^{2}\Theta_{U}^{2}=4B_{*}H_{U}\Theta_{U}\implies H_{U}^{3}\Theta_{U}=4B_{*}\Theta_{U}(H_{U}-\Theta_{U})

proving the claimed relations.
∎

5.
Generators in terms of tautological classes

We use the tautological classes as defined in §
2.4
to express the generators of
A^{*}(\tilde{U}(2,d,2))
as presented in Theorem
3.4
and Theorem
4.13
.

5.1.
Even Degree

We follow the notation as established in §
3
.
As shown in Theorem
4.13
,
A^{*}(\tilde{U}(2,2d,2))
is generated by the cycles
\nu,\tilde{Z}_{d}
in codimension
1
.
Hence, it is enough to express the generators in Theorem
2.8
, which are tautological, in terms of
\nu,\tilde{Z}_{d}
.

We further divide our computations based on the parity of
d
. However, in either case, we shall obtain the same expression in Remark
18
.

Lemma 5.1
.

Put
\xi_{i}:=(1\times\tau_{i,3})^{*}\xi\in Pic(\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}J_{*}(i))
for
i=0,1
and let
\pi_{PJ},\pi_{CP},\pi_{CJ}
be the projections from
\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\times_{M_{*}}J_{*}(0)
onto
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\times_{M_{*}}J_{*}(0),\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}),\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}J_{*}(0)
, respectively.

Using (
1
), the pullback

(f\times 1)^{*}:Pic(|2\Theta_{*}|_{\pi}\times_{M_{*}}J_{*}(0))\rightarrow Pic(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\times_{M_{*}}J_{*}(0))

is injective. Furthermore,

(f\times 1)^{*}(\nu-\dfrac{1}{8}\tilde{Z}_{0})=(f\times 1)^{*}(\pi_{\Theta}^{*}\zeta-2\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{0})=\pi_{PJ*}(c_{2}(\pi_{CP}^{*}(\mathcal{P}\otimes(1\times p_{J})^{*}\xi_{1}^{-1})\otimes\pi_{CJ}^{*}(\xi_{0}))).

Proof.

The bundle
\mathcal{P}
is determined by an extension class on
\mathscr{C}_{J}\times_{J_{*}(1)}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})
. We have the exact sequence

0\rightarrow\pi_{P(\mathcal{E})}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{P(\mathcal{E})}(1)\rightarrow\mathcal{P}\rightarrow(1\times p_{J})^{*}\xi_{1}^{2}\rightarrow 0.

Thus, we have

c(\mathcal{P}\otimes(1\times p_{J})^{*}\xi_{1}^{-1})=(1+\pi_{P(\mathcal{E})}^{*}\mathcal{O}(1)-(1\times p_{J})^{*}\xi_{1})(1+(1\times p_{J})^{*}\xi_{1})\implies

(19)

\displaystyle\pi_{PJ*}(c_{2}(\pi_{CP}^{*}(\mathcal{P}\otimes(1\times p_{J})^{*}\xi_{1}^{-1})\otimes\pi_{CJ}^{*}(\xi_{0})))=\pi_{PJ*}((\pi_{P(\mathcal{E})}^{*}\mathcal{O}(1)-\pi_{CP}^{*}(1\times p_{J})^{*}\xi_{1}+\pi_{CJ}^{*}\xi_{0})\times

\displaystyle(\pi_{CP}^{*}(1\times p_{J})^{*}\xi_{1}+\pi_{CJ}^{*}\xi_{0})).

Applying arguments analogous to Theorem
4.4
, we have

\displaystyle\pi_{PJ*}(\pi_{CP}^{*}(1\times p_{J})^{*}c_{1}(\xi_{1})^{2})=-2\pi_{P(\mathcal{E})}^{*}p_{J}^{*}\Theta_{1},\pi_{PJ*}(\pi_{CJ}^{*}c_{1}(\xi_{0})^{2})=-2\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{0},

\displaystyle\pi_{PJ*}(\pi_{CP}^{*}(1\times p_{J})^{*}c_{1}(\xi_{1}))=1,\pi_{PJ*}(\pi_{CJ}^{*}c_{1}(\xi_{0}))=0\in A^{1}(P(\mathcal{E})\times_{M_{*}}J_{*}(0))\implies

\displaystyle\pi_{PJ*}(c_{2}(\pi_{CP}^{*}(\mathcal{P}\otimes(1\times p_{J})^{*}\xi_{1}^{-1})\otimes\pi_{CJ}^{*}(\xi_{0})))=\pi_{P(\mathcal{E})}^{*}\mathcal{O}(1)+2\pi_{P(\mathcal{E})}^{*}p_{J}^{*}\Theta_{1}-2\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{0}.

The embedding
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\hookrightarrow JU_{*}
in Theorem
3.1
was given by the injection

(20)

\mathcal{E}\otimes L_{J}^{\vee}\otimes L_{\Theta_{*}}^{2}\simeq\mathcal{F}^{\vee}\hookrightarrow\pi^{*}\pi_{*}L_{\Theta_{*}}^{2}.

Let
\zeta
be the relative hyperplane divisor on
\mathbb{P}(\mathscr{E})
. We have

f^{*}\zeta=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})}(1)+p_{J}^{*}(c_{1}(L_{J})-2\Theta_{1})=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})}(1)+2p_{J}^{*}\Theta_{1}.

by Lemma
3.1
. Plugging these values in (
19
) and using (
7
), we get

\pi_{PJ*}(c_{2}(\pi_{CP}^{*}(\mathcal{P}\otimes(1\times p_{J})^{*}\xi_{1}^{-1})\otimes\pi_{CJ}^{*}(\xi_{0})))=\pi_{P(\mathcal{E})}^{*}f^{*}\zeta-2\pi_{J}^{*}\Theta_{0}=(f\otimes 1)^{*}(\nu-\dfrac{1}{8}\tilde{Z}_{0}).

The injectivity of
(f\times 1)^{*}
on Picard groups follows from (
20
).
∎

Remark 18
.

The Poincaré bundle on
\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\times_{M_{*}}J_{*}(0)
defining the map
f\otimes 1:\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\times J_{*}(0)\rightarrow\tilde{U}(2,0,2)
is given by
\pi_{CP}^{*}(\mathcal{P}\otimes(1\times p_{J})^{*}\xi_{1}^{-1})\otimes\pi_{CJ}^{*}(\xi_{0})
. Hence, we have

(f\otimes 1)^{*}(\nu-\dfrac{1}{8}\tilde{Z}_{0})=(f\otimes 1)^{*}\kappa_{-1,0,1}\implies\nu-\dfrac{1}{8}\tilde{Z}_{0}=\kappa_{-1,0,1}.

Furthermore, we have

c_{1}(\mathcal{P}\otimes(1\times p_{J})^{*}\xi_{1}^{-1}\otimes\pi_{CJ}^{*}\xi_{i})=\pi_{P(\mathcal{E})}^{*}\mathcal{O}(1)+2\pi_{CJ}^{*}\xi_{i}\in A^{1}(\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}0}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\times_{M_{*}}J_{*}(i))\quad\forall i\in\mathbb{Z}.

Theorem 5.1
.

Let
\Xi,\Theta
be the generators of
Pic(U(2,2d,2))
as described in Theorem
2.8
. For
d\in\{0,1\}
, we have

\Theta=-\nu-\dfrac{3+2d-4\lfloor\dfrac{d+1}{2}\rfloor}{8}{\tilde{Z}_{d}},\Xi=-\dfrac{1}{2}\tilde{Z}_{d}\in A^{1}(U(2,2d,2)).

Proof.

Applying the Grothendieck-Riemann-Roch theorem, we have

\Lambda(0,1,0)=\dfrac{1}{2}(\kappa_{-1,2,0}-\kappa_{0,1,0}),\Lambda(1,1,0)=\dfrac{1}{2}(\kappa_{-1,2,0}+\kappa_{0,1,0}),\Lambda(0,0,1)=\Lambda(0,1,0)-\kappa_{-1,0,1}.

By Theorem
2.10
, we have the following:

•

\Xi=\kappa_{-1,2,0}-2\kappa_{0,1,0}\in Im(\det_{1}^{*})
for
d=1

•

\Xi=\kappa_{-1,2,0}\in Im(\det_{0}^{*})
for
d=0
.

Using the Poincaré bundle
\xi_{d}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}J_{*}(d)
, where
\xi_{d}:=(1\times\tau_{d,3})^{*}\xi
.
Applying Theorem
4.4
, we get

\xi_{d}^{2}=-\pi_{C}^{*}K_{\pi}.\pi_{J_{*}(d)}^{*}\Theta_{d}\in A^{2}(\mathscr{C}_{*}\times_{M_{*}}J_{*}(d)).

Applying (
7
) for
d=0,1
, we get

\Xi=-2det_{d}^{*}\tau_{2d,1}^{*}(\Theta_{*})=-\dfrac{1}{2}\tilde{Z}_{d}.

For
d
odd, we fix
d=1
and apply Theorem
3.4
and Remark
18
to get

\Theta=\Lambda(0,0,1)=-\Theta_{gen}=-\nu-\dfrac{1}{8}\tilde{Z}_{1},

which proves the claimed expression for
\Theta
.

For
d
even, we fix
d=0
and apply Lemma
5.1
to get

\Theta=\Lambda(0,0,1)+\Lambda(1,1,0)=\kappa_{-1,2,0}-\kappa_{-1,0,1}=-\nu-\dfrac{3}{8}\tilde{Z}_{0}.

∎

5.2.
Odd Degree

We follow the notation in §
4
.
As established in Theorem
4.13
and Theorem
2.7
, the rings
S^{*}(\tilde{U}(2,3,2))\simeq A^{*}(U(2,3,2))\simeq A^{*}(\mathcal{U}(2,3,2))
are generated by
H_{U},\Theta_{U},\text{ and }B_{*}
.

Theorem 5.2
.

Let
\pi_{\mathscr{U}}:\mathscr{C}_{2}\times_{\mathcal{M}_{2}}\mathscr{U}(2,3,2)\rightarrow\mathscr{U}(2,3,2)
be the projection map.
We have

B_{*}=\dfrac{1}{2}\left(\dfrac{1}{2}(\kappa_{-1,2,0}-\kappa_{0,1,0})-\kappa_{-1,0,1}\right)^{2}-\dfrac{1}{2}\left(\dfrac{\kappa_{-1,3,0}}{3}-{\kappa_{-1,1,1}}-\dfrac{\kappa_{0,2,0}}{2}+{\kappa_{0,0,1}}\right)\in A^{2}(\mathcal{U}(2,3,2)).

Proof.

Following a similar argument to that of
[
24
, Lemma 6.4]
, we apply Grothendieck-Riemann-Roch to
\mathcal{E}_{3}
and the map
\pi_{\mathscr{U}}

\displaystyle ch(\pi_{\mathscr{U}!}\mathcal{E}_{3})=\pi_{\mathscr{U}*}\left(ch(\mathcal{E}_{3})\left(1-\dfrac{K_{\pi_{\mathscr{U}}}}{2}\right)\right)\implies c_{1}(\pi_{\mathscr{U}*}\mathcal{E}_{3})=\dfrac{1}{2}(\kappa_{-1,2,0}-\kappa_{0,1,0})-\kappa_{-1,0,2},

\displaystyle B_{*}=-c_{2}(R^{1}\pi_{\mathscr{U}*}\mathcal{E}_{3})=\dfrac{1}{2}\left(\dfrac{1}{2}(\kappa_{-1,2,0}-\kappa_{0,1,0})-\kappa_{-1,0,1}\right)^{2}-\dfrac{1}{2}\left(\dfrac{\kappa_{-1,3,0}}{3}-{\kappa_{-1,1,1}}-\dfrac{\kappa_{0,2,0}}{2}+{\kappa_{0,0,1}}\right)\in A^{2}(\mathcal{U}(2,3,2))

since
c_{1}(R^{1}\pi_{\mathscr{U}*}\mathcal{E}_{3})=0,c_{2}(\pi_{\mathscr{U}*}\mathcal{E}_{3})=0
. The tautological cycle on the right hand side can be shown to be in the image of
\nu_{2,3}^{*}
by Theorem
2.10
.
∎

Theorem

(
[
24
, Lemma 6.4]
)
.

The universal
\Theta
-divisor
\Theta_{U}\in A^{1}(\tilde{U}(2,3,2))
is given by

\Theta_{U}=\dfrac{1}{2}\left(3\kappa_{0,1,0}-\kappa_{-1,2,0}\right)\in A^{1}(\tilde{U}(2,3,2)).

We compute the chern classes of the Poincaré bundle as described in §
4.3
.

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
\sigma_{E}:=\sigma_{\pi}|_{E_{\sigma}}:E_{\sigma}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}_{J}
be the restriction of
\sigma_{\pi}
, and
\pi_{\mathscr{C}_{J}}:\mathscr{C}_{J}\times_{J_{*}(3)}\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}_{J},\pi_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}}:\mathscr{C}_{J}\times_{J_{*}(3)}\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}\rightarrow\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}
be the projection maps.

The chern classes of the bundle
\mathcal{E}^{1}
defined in Theorem
4.3
are given by

c_{1}(\mathcal{E}^{1})=\pi_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}}^{*}(\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}-E_{\sigma})+\pi_{\mathscr{C}_{J}}^{*}\xi,c_{2}(\mathcal{E}^{1})=\pi_{\mathscr{C}_{J}}^{*}\xi\pi_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}}^{*}(\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}-E_{\sigma})+A

where
A:=(1\times i_{\sigma})_{*}(1\times\sigma_{E})^{*}\Delta_{J}
and
\Delta_{J}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{C}_{J}\times_{J_{*}(3)}\mathscr{C}_{J}
is the diagonal embedding of
\mathscr{C}_{J}
.

Proof.

The bunde
\mathcal{E}^{1}
is defined using the following exact sequences

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow\mathcal{E}^{1}\rightarrow(1\times\sigma_{\pi})^{*}\mathcal{E}^{0}\rightarrow(1\times i_{\sigma})_{*}(1\times\sigma_{E})^{*}(\mathcal{O}(\Delta_{J})\otimes\pi_{2}^{*}\mathcal{O}(1)|_{j(\mathscr{C}_{J})})\rightarrow 0,

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow\pi_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}}^{*}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}_{\xi}}(1)\rightarrow(1\times\sigma_{\pi})^{*}\mathcal{E}^{0}\rightarrow\pi_{\mathscr{C}_{J}}^{*}\xi\rightarrow 0.

Let
\mathcal{F}:=(1\times i_{\sigma})_{*}(1\times\sigma_{E})^{*}(\mathcal{O}(\Delta_{J})\otimes\pi_{E_{\sigma}}^{*}(K_{\sigma}\otimes\xi_{\sigma}))
where
\pi_{E_{\sigma}}:\mathscr{C}_{J}\times_{J_{*}(3)}E_{\sigma}\rightarrow E_{\sigma}
be the projection map. We have

\displaystyle c(\mathcal{E}^{1})=c(\mathcal{F})^{-1}(1\times\sigma_{\pi})^{*}c(\mathcal{E}^{0})=c(\mathcal{F})^{-1}(1+\pi_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}}^{*}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J})(1+\pi_{\mathscr{C}_{J}}^{*}\xi)=1+\pi_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}}^{*}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}+\pi_{\mathscr{C}_{J}}^{*}\xi-c_{1}(\mathcal{F})-

\displaystyle c_{1}(\mathcal{F})(\pi_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}}^{*}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}+\pi_{\mathscr{C}_{J}}^{*}\xi)-c_{2}(\mathcal{F})+c_{1}(\mathcal{F})^{2}+\pi_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}}^{*}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}\pi_{\mathscr{C}_{J}}^{*}\xi.

Applying the Grothendieck-Riemann-Roch Theorem to the embedding
(1\times i_{\sigma})
, we get

\displaystyle ch(\mathcal{F})=(1\times i_{\sigma})_{*}\left((1\times\sigma_{E})^{*}ch(\Delta_{J}\otimes\pi_{E_{\sigma}}^{*}\sigma^{*}(K_{\pi_{J}}\otimes\xi))\pi_{E_{\sigma}}^{*}td(\mathcal{N}_{E_{\sigma}/\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}})^{-1}\right)\implies c_{1}(\mathcal{F})=\pi_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}}^{*}E_{\sigma}\implies

\displaystyle c_{2}(\mathcal{F})=\dfrac{1}{2}\pi_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}}^{*}E_{\sigma}^{2}-(1\times i_{\sigma})_{*}(1\times\sigma_{E})^{*}\Delta_{J}-(1\times i_{\sigma})_{*}\pi_{E_{\sigma}}^{*}\sigma^{*}(K_{\pi_{J}}+\xi)+\dfrac{1}{2}(1\times i_{\sigma})_{*}\pi_{E_{\sigma}}^{*}\mathcal{N}_{E_{\sigma}/\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}}=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2}\pi_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}}^{*}E_{\sigma}^{2}-A-\pi_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}}^{*}(K_{\sigma}+\xi_{\sigma})+\dfrac{1}{2}\pi_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}}^{*}i_{\sigma*}\mathcal{O}_{E_{\sigma}}(E_{\sigma})=\pi_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}}^{*}(E_{\sigma}^{2}-K_{\sigma}-\xi_{\sigma})-A.

Plugging these values back into the expression for
c(\mathcal{E}^{1})
, we get

c_{1}(\mathcal{E}^{1})=\pi_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}}^{*}(\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}-E_{\sigma})+\pi_{\mathscr{C}_{J}}^{*}\xi\text{ and }

c_{2}(\mathcal{E}^{1})=\pi_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}}^{*}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}\pi_{\mathscr{C}_{J}}^{*}\xi-\pi_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}}^{*}E_{\sigma}(\pi_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}}^{*}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}+\pi_{\mathscr{C}_{J}}^{*}\xi)+\pi_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}}^{*}E_{\sigma}^{2}+A+\pi_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}}^{*}(K_{\sigma}+\xi_{\sigma}-E_{\sigma}^{2}).

Simplifying the expression, we get
c_{2}(\mathcal{E}^{1})=\pi_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}}^{*}\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}\pi_{\mathscr{C}_{J}}^{*}\xi+A-\pi_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}}^{*}E_{\sigma}\pi_{\mathscr{C}_{J}}^{*}\xi
.
∎

Remark 19
.

Lemma
5.2
can be further verified by applying
\Phi_{\xi}^{*}(B_{*})=\sigma_{\pi}^{*}(H_{J}^{2}+H_{J}\Theta_{\xi}+\dfrac{1}{2}\Theta_{\xi}^{2})-K_{\sigma},
and calculating the associated
\kappa
-classes in
A^{*}(\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi})
using Lemma
5.2
.

Theorem 5.3
.

We have the following expression of
H_{U}
in terms of tautological classes

H_{U}=\dfrac{1}{2}\left(4\kappa_{-1,0,1}-\kappa_{-1,2,0}\right)\in A^{1}(\tilde{U}(2,3,2)).

Proof.

The claimed tautological class is in the image of
\nu_{2,3}^{*}
by Theorem
2.10
since

\pi_{S*}(4c_{2}(\mathcal{E}_{S})-c_{1}(\mathcal{E}_{S})^{2})=\pi_{S*}(4c_{2}(\mathcal{E}_{S}\otimes\pi_{S}^{*}\mathcal{L})-c_{1}(\mathcal{E}_{S}\otimes\pi_{S}^{*}\mathcal{L})^{2})

for any family
\mathcal{E}_{S}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}_{S}\xrightarrow{\pi_{S}}S
and
\mathcal{L}\in Pic(S)
.

Using Lemma
5.2
and Lemma
4.15
, we have

\displaystyle\pi_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}*}A=\pi_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}*}(1\times i_{\sigma})_{*}(1\times\sigma_{E})^{*}\Delta_{J}=\pi_{E_{\sigma}*}(1\times\sigma_{E})^{*}\Delta_{J}=E_{\sigma}\implies

\displaystyle\pi_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}*}(4c_{2}(\mathcal{E}^{1})-c_{1}(\mathcal{E}^{1})^{2})=4\pi_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}*}(\pi_{\mathscr{C}_{J}}^{*}\xi\pi_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}}^{*}(\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}-E_{\sigma})+A)-\pi_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}*}(\pi_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\xi}}^{*}(\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}-E_{\sigma})+\pi_{\mathscr{C}_{J}}^{*}\xi)^{2}

\displaystyle=6\sigma_{\pi}^{*}H_{J}+2\sigma_{\pi}^{*}\Theta_{\xi}-2E_{\sigma}=2\Phi_{\xi}^{*}(H_{U}).

The theorem now follows from the injectivity of
\Phi_{\xi}^{*}
.
∎

Corollary 9
.

Similar computations also yield the following relation in
A^{2}(\mathscr{U}(2,3,2))

4\kappa_{0,0,1}-\kappa_{0,2,0}=\dfrac{1}{2}\left(4\kappa_{-1,0,1}-\kappa_{-1,2,0}\right)^{2}.
